\begin{abstract}
\textbf{A preserved statistic is not preserved suppression.} No test that admits a composition because one constituent already suppresses the attack can identify whether the downstream mechanism caused its success. Federated learning composes defenses never designed together, so requiring the downstream \emph{statistic} to survive the upstream stage is the natural repair. It fails structurally rather than statistically: the gate selects on the very outcome to be explained, so the comparison identification needs is missing at any $n$. The repair is a design, not a better criterion: hold the downstream defense and the attack fixed and intervene on the upstream transform. Ignoring this costs not power but the sign. On one cell the outcome-gated design moves $-0.273$ where the within-defense design moves $+0.125$ at $n{=}20$, both $95\%$ intervals excluding zero. The two designs disagree on five of seven cells, and the reversal replicates on CIFAR-100. Two dissociations say why, with the adversary's coefficient pinned so the attack cannot weaken. Krum's decision flips in up to $80\%$ of rounds while it admits no adversary at all and suppression stays inside a pre-registered $\pm0.15$ margin. Conversely \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical while ASR falls $0.173$. The negative replicates in a second invariance class and outside the theory's rescaling family. We do \emph{not} claim that admission predicts security. And no gate of this shape can certify \emph{emergent} suppression, so recall is capped structurally, our own lowest-ASR composition the witness.
\end{abstract}

\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:intro}
% Round 58, reviewer item #1. The words are the ones that were here; what changed is that the four
% bolded run-in heads ("The question." / "Why the natural answer fails." / "What replaces it." /
% "Whether it matters.") became paragraph breaks, so a reader meets one idea per paragraph instead of
% four in an eleven-line block. No number and no clause was dropped.
\noindent A composition of two defenses suppresses an attack. \textbf{Is the downstream defense's mechanism what suppressed it?} Everything below is an answer to that one question.

% design, measured facts, FL grounding; it is now question, FL grounding, measured facts, evaluation
% failure, corrected design, which is the review's requested chain (FL problem -> evaluation failure ->
% causal explanation -> corrected design). :85 is unmoved and unedited; the review says keep it.
% Three things changed inside the moved text and nothing was dropped. (1) The second measured fact used
% to read "a composition can leave a downstream statistic bit-identical while the suppression that
% follows it moves", which is true and unusable: both dissociations are now named with their numbers
% here, where the story needs them, rather than only inside contribution item (3) at :114. Those numbers
% are tab:channels rows 1 and 6 and are stated again at :305 and :373. (2) "committed (non-adaptive)"
% is added at the first statement of the finding: every C1-bearing claim in the paper assumes a
% committed adversary, and the caveat previously first surfaced on p6. box:scope now carries it too.
% (3) The FL grounding paragraph gains \noindent, matching the four paragraphs it now sits among.
\noindent Federated learning~\citep{mcmahan2017communication} is vulnerable to data poisoning~\citep{chen2017targeted,bagdasaryan2020backdoor}, and an arms race has produced defenses~\citep{fung2020foolsgold,pillutla2022robust,yin2018byzantine,blanchard2017machine} with accuracy/robustness trade-offs and no dominant member (Appendix~\ref{app:related}). \emph{Can we compose them for stronger robustness?} Composition does not even commute: FoolsGold$\to$CoordMedian suppresses a pixel backdoor to ASR $0.099$ while the reverse reaches $0.732$, that order collapsing to FoolsGold alone bit-identically, since a rank aggregator emits one vector rather than per-client updates.

\noindent Two measured facts put the question there in the first place. Two defenses that each fail alone can jointly suppress a committed (non-adaptive) attack: FoolsGold$\to$RFA reaches ASR $0.093$ where FoolsGold alone reaches $0.732$ and RFA alone $0.845$. And the link an evaluator leans on breaks in both directions: Krum's selection flips in $0.733$ of rounds while its admission is unchanged and its suppression does not follow ($|\Delta|{=}0.026$), while \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every rung and its ASR nonetheless \emph{falls} $0.173$ (both drawn in Fig.~\ref{fig:story}, App.~\ref{app:story}).

\noindent The natural test admits a composition because one constituent already suppresses the attack. \textbf{That gate cannot answer the question at any sample size}, structurally rather than statistically, because it removes the counterfactual support identification requires.

\noindent Replacing that gate reverses a \emph{sign}: $-0.273$ outcome-gated against $+0.125$ within-defense on one cell at $n{=}20$. What replaces it is a design rather than a better test: hold the downstream defense and the attack fixed and intervene on the upstream transform, instead of comparing across a menu of defenses. The reason for the disagreement is mechanical: the upstream transform can weaken the attack itself, so an outcome-gated comparison can credit the downstream defense with a fall in ASR that attenuation upstream produced.

\textbf{Why the question is sharper without a trusted coordinator.} A contributor clips, a scheduler reweights by reputation, and whoever merges aggregates robustly: three independently developed mechanisms, nobody owning the stack, so the only check at each seam is that the next stage still reads what it expects. We claim no transfer beyond the controlled setting we study (p\pageref{box:scope}), though the seam, rather than the scale, is what the problem is about.

% Round 58, reviewer items #1/#6/#11. This preamble used to recite the five preservation levels in
% prose before Def.~\ref{def:levels} states them, so a reader met P1--P5 twice before either
% mention was usable. The recital is deleted, not moved: :174--:185 is its home, and "suppression
% = max-committed ASR, never the statistic or the decision" is fixed at :187 and again at :267.
\noindent\textbf{Contributions.} We propose no new defense, and the question is scientific rather than operational: whether an evaluation can identify that a mechanism \emph{caused} an observed suppression, not whether a deployment can detect malicious clients. Preservation is a hierarchy, and statistic-level invariance is not a causal surrogate for security-level preservation (§\ref{sec:framework}).

% Round 61, reviewer item #7. The list was already THREE items; what was wrong is that each item was a

% were reachable only by reading a bullet. They are now stated in the story at :91 with their names and
% numbers, and each item here is one claim plus the pointer to where it is made. Nothing left the paper:
% the recall bound's "total rather than partial" is Cor.~\ref{cor:zero_recall}'s own statement and
% §\ref{app:prioritization}'s subject, the positivity concession is now a titled paragraph in
% §\ref{sec:testability} where a reviewer can point at it, and "the two are not the same intervention"
% is :306's whole subject. 2122 chars to ~1150, which is what funds this round's additions.
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt,leftmargin=*,label=\textbf{(\arabic*)}]
\item \textbf{Two impossibility results (§\ref{sec:testability}).} An inherited-suppression gate selects on the very outcome to be explained, so one cell of the relevant $2\times2$ is empty for \emph{every} menu and no sample size repairs it (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}). Emergent suppression fails such a gate by definition, so any evaluator using one accepts a blind spot for emergent synergy (Prop.~\ref{prop:emergent}, Cor.~\ref{cor:zero_recall}).
\item \textbf{The design it forces, and a sign reversal (§\ref{sec:protocol}--§\ref{sec:targeted}).} Every clause of the design is forced by a term in the proposition (Cor.~\ref{cor:within_design}), and the screen that follows is a consequence we price rather than a third contribution (§\ref{sec:strategies}).
\item \textbf{The preservation hierarchy, and the two links that break (§\ref{sec:framework}, §\ref{sec:targeted}).} Both breaks are the two measured facts above, at fixed downstream defense and attack; the demonstration is a sign inversion and two exact invariances, not small $n{=}5$ mean differences. Which level a composition preserves is settled by algebra before any run, and is reusable without our screen (Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent The paper is a methodological correction to how composed defenses are evaluated, and we report our own refutations as such: the cross-arm admission ordering we had predicted collapses, and our own lowest-ASR composition is a false negative of our own criterion.

\section{The Testability Boundary: Why the Gated Test Fails}
\label{sec:testability}

\emph{Why can we not simply compare many defense pairs?} The natural test keeps pairs whose constituents already suppress the attack and asks whether the composition preserves it. That is exactly where identification is lost. \emph{Screen}, throughout, names a rule for choosing which compositions are worth running, never a predictor of security. The other words are fixed too: \emph{gate} is the eligibility condition below, \emph{criterion} our own composability rule, \emph{protocol} §\ref{sec:protocol}'s four clauses, \emph{instruments} Mode~S and score-only Krum. \emph{Whose protocol is this?} It is the gate our own criterion applies, which is why the boundary costs us our own lowest-ASR composition (§\ref{sec:strategies}).


\noindent \textbf{Intuition.} An admitted pair's downstream defense is guaranteed to suppress the attack alone, so no admitted pair exists in which that defense fails alone while the composition succeeds. That missing comparison is the only one that would credit the suppression to the mechanism rather than to the downstream defense's own strength. Sample size is beside the point: the required counterfactual is absent by construction.

\begin{proposition}[Outcome-gated comparisons cannot identify the upstream defense's contribution]
\label{prop:identification}
Let a screen certify $(d_1 \to d_2)$ against attack $a$ only if both
(i) an \textbf{eligibility gate} $E(d_1,d_2,a) := \min_{d \in \{d_1,d_2\}} M(d,a) < \theta$ holds, where $M$ and $\theta$ are the \emph{same} outcome metric and threshold the screen predicts for the composition, and
(ii) a \textbf{mechanism condition} $P(d_1,d_2)$ holds, determined by the two defenses' definitions alone.
Suppose the screen is applied only where the downstream mechanism is load-bearing, $M(d_1,a) \ge \theta$. Consider estimating $P$'s contribution to $M(d_1 \to d_2, a)$ from contrasts across a defense menu $\mathcal{D}$. Then every certified pair satisfies $M(d_2,a) < \theta$, so of the four cells of $\{P,\neg P\} \times \{M(d_2,a) < \theta,\ M(d_2,a) \ge \theta\}$ the certified set occupies at most two, and the cell needed to separate $P$ from $d_2$'s standalone effectiveness is empty by the gate, for every $\mathcal{D}$. No contrast varying $d_2$ identifies $P$'s contribution. Identification requires holding $d_2$, $a$ and therefore $E$ fixed while intervening on the upstream transform: \textbf{the admissible designs are within-defense.}
\end{proposition}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
% Round 58: 0.82\linewidth, not \linewidth. Native 405.963 x 127.312 pt, so full width renders
% ~124pt tall and the round needed ~22pt of it back. Nothing cropped or redrawn, and this PDF
% extracts no text under pdftotext, so legibility is a pixel check and not a grep.
\includegraphics[width=0.82\linewidth]{figures/identification_dag.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{Not ordinary collider bias but a structural \emph{positivity} violation, which is why the remedy is a design and not an estimator.} \textbf{(a)} Ordinary collider bias, where every cell stays populated and adjustment recovers the estimand in principle. \textbf{(b)} The inherited-suppression gate makes $G$ \emph{deterministic} in $S$, so the column the identifying contrast needs is empty and no sample size restores it (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}). \textbf{(c)} The repair: hold $d_2$ and $a$ fixed, intervene on $T$, and run $T{=}\mathrm{id}$ as the control whatever its outcome (Cor.~\ref{cor:within_design}; App.~\ref{app:mapping}).}
\label{fig:identification}
\end{figure}

\noindent \textbf{The empty cell is the whole of it}, and it is empty by the gate rather than by bad luck: comparing the two occupied cells varies $d_2$ and so changes the conditioning event instead of intervening, while the contrast that would credit $P$ lives in the column the gate forbids.

\noindent \textbf{Nothing in this statement is about federated learning.} $M$ is any outcome metric, $\theta$ any threshold, $P$ any condition on the two mechanisms' definitions, and $\mathcal{D}$ any menu; App.~\ref{app:mapping} substitutes our protocol into each, term by term. The proof is short (App.~\ref{app:boundary}). What takes work is deciding $P$, which §\ref{sec:framework} does algebraically and before any run.

\paragraph{What we estimate instead.} Fix a downstream defense $d_2$ and a committed attack $a$. The \textbf{treatment} $T$ is the upstream per-client transformation applied before $d_2$ aggregates, with $T{=}\mathrm{id}$ the control that is bit-identical to $d_2$ standalone. The \textbf{outcome} $Y$ is $\mathrm{ASR}(T,d_2,a)$. The \textbf{mechanism-preservation variable} $P\in\{0,1\}$ records whether $T$ preserves the statistic $d_2$ reads, at whichever level is at issue. Which of the five levels is at issue is §\ref{sec:framework}'s subject; the boundary below holds for any such condition. $P$ is a deterministic function of $T$ and $d_2$'s definition, not a measured covariate. The causal quantity is
\[
\tau(T)\;=\;\mathbb{E}\!\left[Y \mid \mathrm{do}(T),\,d_2,\,a\right]\;-\;\mathbb{E}\!\left[Y \mid \mathrm{do}(T{=}\mathrm{id}),\,d_2,\,a\right].
\]

% Round 61: split out of the paragraph above, which ran 1373 chars across a display. Nothing is cut;
% the seam is where the subject changes, from what the estimand IS to what it deliberately is not.
% The bold opener is a run-in head, so the long-bold rule still exempts it.
\textbf{We do not estimate the causal effect of $P$ itself.} $P$ is not manipulable apart from $T$, being a property of the pair, so ``the effect of preservation'' names no intervention. We estimate the effect of intervening on the upstream transformation while holding $d_2$ and $a$ fixed, and what we report about $P$ is whether $\tau(T)\neq 0$ occurs while $P$ holds, and whether $\tau(T){=}0$ occurs while $P$ fails. Both occur (§\ref{sec:targeted}), which is what refutes $P$ as a surrogate for $Y$.

\paragraph{Is this not ordinary collider bias?} Half of it is, and that half is textbook~\citep{elwert2014endogenous,pearl2009causality}: we claim no credit for it. What the gate adds is a structural \emph{positivity} violation rather than a confounding one (Fig.~\ref{fig:identification}b), while reading as a reasonableness filter. The gate also earns its keep: it stops the screen certifying pairs no constituent suppresses, so wherever our conditions certify a pair, a low composition ASR is attributable to $d_2$'s standalone strength rather than to preservation, the reading we held ourselves (§\ref{sec:dose_response}).

% Round 61, review §21.2, which calls this "probably the most important reviewer objection" and asks for
% a place a reviewer can point at. The concession itself is not new -- the paragraph above concedes the
% textbook half, and contribution item (1) says "half of the first result is textbook positivity" -- but
% it was a clause inside two other arguments and had no home of its own. Deliberately plain: no \textbf
% at all, because the bold ratchet is at its cap and a defence written in bold reads as protesting.
\paragraph{Why this is more than a standard positivity violation.} The violation itself is standard; four things around it are not. The estimand is specific to composed defenses, being which \emph{level} of a preservation hierarchy an upstream transform leaves intact, and that hierarchy has no analogue outside this setting. The gate that empties the cell is a documented convention, not an analyst's slip, and its prevalence is measured, not asserted: $6$ of $59$ coded composed-defense papers ($10.2\%$) gate attribution on the composed outcome while running no identifying contrast; $46$ ($78\%$) do run one (App.~\ref{app:audit}). A low rate was pre-registered as outcome (ii), so our decline to call the gate common stays, evidenced not hedged.

\noindent The design that repairs it has to be built, because holding the downstream defense fixed still leaves a channel that bypasses the statistic and reaches the outcome, and that channel must be closed by construction. And the consequence is measured rather than argued: preserving what a defense computes, and even its decision, leaves suppression free to move here, and a real selection methodology, ours, reverses the sign of its own conclusion once the gated comparison is replaced.

\noindent \textbf{A screen of this shape fails a second, independent way}, bounding what it can find rather than what it can be validated on.

\begin{proposition}[Coverage: inherited-suppression screens cannot certify emergent synergy]
\label{prop:emergent}
Call $(d_1 \to d_2)$'s suppression of $a$ \textbf{emergent} if $M(d_1 \to d_2, a) < \theta$ while $\min_{d \in \{d_1,d_2\}} M(d,a) \ge \theta$. No emergent composition satisfies the eligibility gate of Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}. Hence, writing $\mathcal{S}$ for the compositions with $M < \theta$, $\mathcal{E} \subseteq \mathcal{S}$ for the emergent ones and $\mathcal{S} \setminus \mathcal{E}$ for the inherited ones, any such screen's recall obeys $\mathrm{recall} \le 1 - |\mathcal{E}|/|\mathcal{S}|$, on every menu and every attack.
\end{proposition}

% Round 61, funding B6 (review §5: Prop. 2 should not carry equal rhetorical weight). 825 chars to ~660.
% The recall arithmetic moves to its existing home: App.~\ref{app:boundary} at :735 states "One emergent
% pair among five low-ASR pairs puts the cap here at $80\%$ against a measured $40\%$" -- the same
% $|\mathcal E|/|\mathcal S|{=}1/5$, spelled out beside the proposition it bounds. What stays here is the
% witness, its numbers, and the reason no screen of this shape can reach $\mathcal E$.
\noindent \textbf{Our own lowest-ASR composition is the witness}: §\ref{sec:intro}'s FG$\to$RFA, at max-committed ASR $0.093$ ($n{=}30$, CI $[0.062,\,0.123]$) against constituents that each fail alone, is emergent and a false negative of our own criterion, which is what puts the bound above the recall we report (App.~\ref{app:boundary}). It splits a menu into \emph{inheritance}, which we screen for, and \emph{emergence}, a search problem we do not solve. A screen reaching $\mathcal{E}$ would have to read the composition's own suppression, the outcome it exists to avoid conditioning on: Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}'s boundary met from the other side. Our blind spot is one condition, C1, so that recall is an \emph{operating point}, not a ceiling (§\ref{sec:strategies}).

\begin{center}
\fbox{\parbox{\dimexpr\linewidth-2\fboxsep-2\fboxrule\relax}{\small
\phantomsection\label{box:scope}
\textbf{What this paper establishes, and what it does not, stated once.}

\emph{Formally.} Neither proposition above contains a dataset, architecture or scale, so what carries
beyond this paper is the design they license and not our screen. Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}
(App.~\ref{app:proofs}) and the invariance classes are proved for positive per-client rescalings into
named rank and geometric aggregators, and bound no ASR.

\emph{Empirically.} We identify the effect of intervening on the upstream transform at fixed downstream
defense and attack, and offer no causal model of FL; on one cell that design change flips the
\emph{sign} of the estimated effect. The experiments are four aggregators of three structural kinds
under two committed attacks on CIFAR-10, three arms replicated, on EMNIST-byclass ($n{=}3$ frozen,
$n{=}5$ pooled), \texttt{resnet18} ($n{=}3$) and CIFAR-100, at $n{=}5$ seeds, $n{=}20$ on the CIFAR-10 sign-reversal cell and on
Krum/scaling's Mode~S leg, $n{=}8$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}.

% Round 61, A9/B9 (review §11 and §21.5). Split rather than compressed: the three DENIALS are now the
% first sentence, so a reader who reads one sentence of this box reads what we do not claim, and one
% denial is ADDED -- "nothing about adaptive robustness". That is the review's §11 ask (move the adaptive
% caveat earlier): it first appeared on p6 at the threat-model paragraph, and this box renders on p5.
% tab:tiers' Formal row already carries the long form ("never adaptive robustness").
% Two PROVENANCE clauses are demoted to the pointers already beside them, each home verified: C0's
% retrospective formulation is (L4) verbatim ("C0 was itself formulated after seeing this suite, so its 7
% exclusions are retrospective"), and "measurement devices rather than defenses" has six homes including
% tab:tiers' Both-instruments row and the Conclusion. The confidentiality/availability clause is NOT
% demoted and is kept word for word: :215 is its only home in either document.
\emph{Not established.} We do \emph{not} establish that admission predicts security, we claim nothing
about adaptive robustness, and we claim nothing about confidentiality or availability. This is a
methodology for identifying what a composition preserves, not for choosing a defense to deploy: the
screen prioritizes what to evaluate, and nothing here is a deployment recommendation. Claims
do not transfer upward either: scope conditions in Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}, evidence tiers in
Table~\ref{tab:tiers}, limits in §\ref{sec:limitations}, (L1)--(L6), and every claim we have withdrawn
or refuted in Table~\ref{tab:status}. The criterion's empirical validation is exploratory (L4), and our
instruments read adversary identity (§\ref{sec:conclusion}).
}}
\end{center}

\section{Which Level a Composition Preserves}
\label{sec:framework}

% Round 58, reviewer item #3: the grouping is a LAYER over the existing indices, never a rename.
% figures/modeS_causal.pdf and figures/story_chain.pdf both bake "P1"--"P5" into their rendered
% labels, so renaming would strand two PDFs (and the modeS generator writes into workshop_paper/,
% which is pinned). The internal/security cut is the paper's own, hoisted from :189, where it says
% admission's content is adversarial influence "rather than $d_2$'s internal operation".
% Round 61, reviewer clarity item #2 ("stop making the reader decode P1--P5 so early") and item #3. The
% opening now says the five senses in ordinary words BEFORE any index, and the centred block below gains
% the plain-word chain as its first line. Nothing is renamed and no (P#) token is added: the count is
% pinned at 16 by the clarity gate, and figures/modeS_causal.pdf and figures/story_chain.pdf bake the
% labels into rendered pixels. Two claims that had their ONLY home in this paragraph are kept verbatim in
% substance: the internal/security cut, and the observable/mechanistic cut one place later. The dropped
% clause is "four increasingly strong forms", which mis-ordered its own chain -- (P1)$\Rightarrow$(P2)
% $\Rightarrow$(P3) makes (P1) the strongest of the three, so the list is not increasing in strength. It
% is ordered from what the defense computes to what the attacker gets, and now says so.
\emph{Preserved in which sense?} The condition $P$ the boundary quantifies over is not one condition but a graded family, and \textbf{which member of it a claim asserts decides whether the claim is checkable at all}. An upstream transform can leave a downstream defense ``the same'' in five senses, ordered from what the defense computes to what the attacker gets: three about the defense's \emph{internal} behaviour, then two about \emph{security} behaviour, what the adversary gets.

\noindent The algebra cuts one place later, everything up to admission being \emph{observable} and attack suppression \emph{mechanistic}. These are five different claims, and preservation at one level does not imply preservation at the next:
\begin{center}\vspace{-5pt}
% Broken by hand into two balanced lines: left to itself the fill breaks inside the last clause and
% orphans `cos_krum (§6.2)' on a near-empty line. Line 1 is the definitional part, line 2 the two
% causal breaks, so the break falls where the semantics already do.
statistic $\to$ ordering $\to$ decision $\to$ admission $\to$ suppression\\
(P1)$\Rightarrow$(P2)$\Rightarrow$(P3) by definition;\\
(P4)$\not\Rightarrow$(P3), witness Krum (§\ref{sec:targeted});\quad (P4)$\not\Rightarrow$(P5), witness \texttt{cos\_krum} (§\ref{sec:targeted_rest}).
\vspace{-5pt}\end{center}
\noindent \textbf{Where that chain breaks is this paper's subject}: the first two implications are logical and need no experiment, the last two are causal and each is settled by the one arm that witnesses its failure. Fig.~\ref{fig:modeS}(a) and Fig.~\ref{fig:story} (App.~\ref{app:story}) draw the same chain.

\noindent \textbf{To instantiate the hierarchy in FL we characterize the invariance classes}, which is where the levels stop being a taxonomy and start being decidable: every per-client defense we consider acts as a positive scalar rescaling $T(u_i) = c_i u_i$, so the algebra fixes which level a given composition sits at, before any run: cosine similarity is exactly invariant, coordinate ordering only under a bounded-ratio condition, and consensus distance and residual magnitude not at all (Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}, App.~\ref{app:proofs}).

\begin{definition}[Preservation levels]
\label{def:levels}
For an upstream transform $T$ and a downstream defense $d_2$ reading statistic $S$:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt,label=(P\arabic*)]
\item \textbf{Value invariance:} $S(T(U)) = S(U)$.
\item \textbf{Ordering invariance:} $S(T(U)) = \varphi(S(U))$ for some $\varphi$ preserving the ordering $S$ induces on clients.
\item \textbf{Decision invariance:} the client or coordinate $d_2$ actually selects is unchanged.
\item \textbf{Adversarial contribution (admission) invariance:} the adversarial input $d_2$ admits into its aggregate is unchanged. \emph{Admission} is the pre-registered term throughout for whether adversarial input survives $d_2$, the support of the admitted mass rather than the mass itself. The mass is influence $\Lambda_a$, which a preserved support leaves free to move (Table~\ref{tab:channels}).
\item \textbf{Attack-suppression preservation:} ASR stays below threshold, for the fixed committed attack the arm is run against.
\end{enumerate}
The screening conditions the levels induce, and their names, are in App.~\ref{app:framework} and~\ref{app:conditions}.
\end{definition}

% Round 61, reviewer clarity item #3, which asks for exactly this as a table with these four terms.
% Two constraints shaped it. (i) No (P#) token is added: the prose it replaces carried (P3), (P4), (P5)
% and so does the table, so the gated count stays at 16, and "statistic" is deliberately unlabelled
% rather than tagged (P1)/(P2). (ii) NO RENAMING. The review also asks that "admission" become something
% more standard; it is the pre-registered term, said so at :266, and renaming it would contradict a frozen
% pre-registration, so the glossary is the answer instead. Influence ($\Lambda_a$) and attenuation cannot
% leave the main text -- $\Delta\Lambda_a$ is a column header of Table~\ref{tab:channels} and attenuation
% is what protocol clause (3) closes -- so they follow the table as the two words that are NOT these four.
\noindent \textbf{Four words, fixed here and used in one sense throughout.}
\begin{center}\small\vspace{-4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
term & what it names \\
\midrule
statistic & what the downstream defense computes from the updates it reads \\
decision & which client or coordinate it selects (P3) \\
admission & whether adversarial input enters its aggregate at all (P4) \\
suppression & whether the attack still succeeds, i.e.\ max-committed ASR (P5) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\vspace{-4pt}
\end{center}

\noindent Two further words are needed, each distinct from those four. \emph{Influence} is $\Lambda_a$, the weight the adversary's direction carries in the aggregate. \emph{Attenuation} is the adversary's own update shrunk upstream, reaching ASR without passing through the statistic at all, and it is the channel an intervention on $T$ must close before either of the other two can be read.

% Round 61, funding B5 (review §21.4: reduce terminology in the main narrative). 708 chars to ~460. The
% three per-aggregator instantiations move to App.~\ref{app:notation}'s $\Lambda_a$ row, which already
% prints exactly them ("weight share, selected-coordinate share, or the selection indicator"), and
% App.~\ref{app:channel_table} measures them one column per defense. The consequence a reader must not
% miss -- four within-family statements, never differenced across defenses -- stays in the body.
\noindent \textbf{Admission (P4) is the first level at which adversarial influence enters the causal graph}, which is why we intervene there rather than on the statistic. It is privileged for its semantics, not for sufficiency. It is a causal intervention variable, not a security metric a deployed defense could compute. It is also instantiated per aggregator (App.~\ref{app:notation}), so our admission result is four within-family statements and we never difference or rank across them.

% Round 61, funding B8: 495 chars to ~330. The $\rho$-only reduction's vacuity is stated in full at :840
% ("A further reduction to $\rho$ alone is vacuous on our runs, and we report it as vacuous") and in (L5);
% the term "mechanism preservation" is fixed where it is defined, §\ref{sec:composability_criterion}, and
% the $m{=}3.24 \to 1.01$ heterogeneity arithmetic is supplement :409 and main :972. The three things a
% reader needs here -- supporting, not security, bounds no ASR -- are said in plain words instead.
\noindent Quantitative margins for the weighting$\to$aggregator case are a \emph{supporting} result and stay in the appendix: they certify the downstream defense's discriminative operation rather than its security behaviour, they bound no ASR, and heterogeneity closes them, so no claim here rests on them (Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}, App.~\ref{app:proofs}; L5).


\section{A Causal Protocol: Intervene Upstream, Not Across Defenses}
\label{sec:protocol}

\emph{If the comparison is inadmissible, what replaces it?} Not a better criterion but a different design, and Prop.~\ref{prop:identification} determines it: do not select across defenses; hold the downstream defense and the attack fixed, intervene on the upstream transformation, and measure the causal channels separately. Writing $T_1$ for the upstream transformation, the estimand is $\Delta_{\mathrm{mech}} = \mathrm{ASR}(T_1^{\mathrm{int}}, d_2, a) - \mathrm{ASR}(T_1^{\mathrm{base}}, d_2, a)$, at fixed $d_2$, fixed attack $a$ and fixed adversarial coefficient share; the clauses below are what hold each of those fixed, and the third is why this is a mechanism contrast and not a dose.

\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt,leftmargin=*,label=(\arabic*)]
\item \textbf{Hold $d_2$ and the attack fixed.} \emph{Forced by} the gate $E(d_1,d_2,a)$: vary $d_2$ or $a$ and the two sides of a contrast are conditioned on different events, so they are not comparable at any sample size.
\item \textbf{Intervene on the upstream transform.} \emph{Forced by} the empty cell: variation in $d_2$ is exactly what the gate turns into missing support, so the intervention has to live on $d_1$.
\item \textbf{Pin the adversarial coefficient channel.} \emph{Forced by} what the proposition does not constrain: nothing prevents $T$ from reaching the outcome without passing through $d_2$'s statistic at all, by attenuating whichever client carries the poison (Lem.~\ref{lem:annihilation}).
\item \textbf{Measure the channels, not only the outcome.} \emph{Forced by} what $P$ asserts: a claim about a mechanism, so a contrast reporting only ASR cannot say which channel carried the effect, and every channel the intervention opens must be reported, including the accidental ones.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent \textbf{Operationally, before any of that}, name the downstream mechanism's invariance class and the level of Def.~\ref{def:levels} the claim asserts (§\ref{sec:framework}; Table~\ref{tab:tiers}'s Formal row names the transforms the algebra does not reach). Following the four clauses reversed the sign of our own conclusion on the same cell (§\ref{sec:targeted}).

\paragraph{Threat model: committed, not adaptive.} Every clause holds the attack fixed, so the protocol establishes attack-suppression preservation under a fixed committed attack and nothing wider, which is why we do not call the object ``security''. One whitebox composition-aware \emph{adaptive} stress test measures that boundary: solving the joint FoolsGold/RFA constraint knowing both stages raises FG$\to$RFA to ASR $0.167 \pm 0.069$, $3.7\times$ its seed-matched $0.045$. \textbf{Inherited robustness is not adaptive robustness}, and a protocol stated against an adaptive adversary is the sharpest thing this framework lacks (App.~\ref{app:secondary}).

\section{Statistic Preservation Does Not Identify Attack-Suppression Preservation}
\label{sec:targeted}

\emph{Does disturbing what a defense reads actually cost suppression?} The claim: it need not, and \textbf{on the same cell the design that says otherwise disagrees in sign}. We adjudicate the implication that losing the decision means losing suppression by oracle intervention on two arms in two invariance classes, not by a general law.

% Round 58, reviewer item #4. The worked example is not new material: Krum under model scaling is
% already this section's subject and already row 1 (with row 6 its control) of tab:channels. What was
% missing is the sentence saying so, which is why a reader could not tell that one cell is followed
% end to end. No number is retyped here; the rows are cited.
% Round 64, page-gate funding: the closing sentence ("Everything below instruments that one cell,
% ending in the design comparison that reverses its sign") is a recital of this section's own lead one
% paragraph above -- :372 states the sign disagreement in bold and in the same words -- and of this
% sentence's own first clause. Both halves keep a home; no claim is left with zero homes.
\noindent \textbf{One cell is carried end to end, and it is Krum under model scaling}: row~1 of Table~\ref{tab:channels}, with row~6 its magnitude-controlled twin.

\paragraph{The instrument, as a counterfactual.} The clean question asks what happens when the downstream statistic changes but the adversary cannot become weaker. \textbf{Mode S} enforces exactly that: it pins every adversary's coefficient at $c{=}1.0$ and spreads only the benign clients over $\rho = e^{2\kappa}$, holding the adversarial coefficient share constant at $0.266667$ to within float32 read-back noise, closing Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}(a)'s attenuation path, so a rise in ASR here cannot be attenuation. §\ref{sec:validation}'s payload-share instrument is the other such device (Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}, App.~\ref{app:targeted_full}).

\paragraph{Two readings of preservation, frozen against each other.} \emph{The statistic reading}: what matters is disturbance of $d_2$'s statistic, read off its \emph{decisions}. \emph{The admission reading}: what matters is the \emph{adversarial input $d_2$ admits}. Mode~S disturbs Krum's selection heavily while leaving that admission untouched, so that arm adjudicates (Table~\ref{tab:targeted}): as $\rho$ climbs to $54.6$ the decision changes in up to $0.80$ of rounds, the adversarial mass admitted in none, and ASR shows no evidence of the predicted increase, staying inside the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin ($|\Delta|{=}0.026<0.15$).

% Round 64: this sentence's closing clause used to read "every equivalence reading in the paper", and
% the ResNet18 arm added this round FALSIFIED it -- that arm's paired 95% interval is [-0.183,+0.101],
% so its m* is 0.183 and it would fail every rung of the ladder including the frozen 0.15. Nothing in
% the build, the hashes or either gate can see a claim going false this way; only re-deriving the new
% arm's interval against the standing claim can. The clause is now scoped to the table it was always
% measured over (the ladder's caption and supplementary.tex's paragraph were ALREADY scoped to the
% comparability arms; the body sentence was the only unscoped site), and the exception is named with
% its number rather than left to the appendix. Disclosure (v) of app:resnet18_dose carries the full
% reading, including that TOST at the 90% interval alpha=.05 reads does reject at p=0.040.
\noindent \textbf{Here practical equivalence is established, not merely consistent}: TOST against that frozen $\pm0.15$ margin, an evaluation-design tolerance conjoined with every rung below $0.5$, not a claim that $15$ ASR points are negligible, gives $p{=}0.0014$ at $n{=}5$, and a pre-registered top-up to $n{=}20$ confirms it at $\Delta{=}-0.010$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.032,+0.012]$ (App.~\ref{app:tost} scores every arm this way, us included). \emph{The margin is not load-bearing} for any reading in Table~\ref{tab:margin_ladder}; \texttt{resnet18}'s $n{=}3$ replication is the one arm that needs it (App.~\ref{app:targeted_full}).

\noindent The statistic reading is refuted: statistic and decision disturbance need not change suppression once the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed. \emph{The zero is a floor here}, Krum admitting no adversary at any rung, so unchanged admission is not by itself decisive, and we claim no per-channel decomposition of $\Delta$ASR (§\ref{app:factorial}).

\noindent \textbf{The table contains its own positive control}, without which ``we moved something and nothing happened'' could not be told from too blunt an instrument: suppression moves in exactly the two arms whose \emph{influence} channel moves, reputation and coord.\ median. Krum changes the decision at $\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.000$ and suppression does not follow, so a decision change is not \emph{sufficient}; \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every rung, also at $\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.000$, yet ASR \emph{falls}, so it is not \emph{necessary} either. % Round 61, page gate: two paragraphs consolidated into one, and every clause of what left has a
% verbatim home in the appendix this paragraph already cited. :1681 names channels (a)--(d) in full,
% with the $0.892$ split into re-selection and rescaling; :1683 states the score-only mechanism; :1685
% records the freeze commit; :1687 gives $-0.023$ against $-0.026$ and the $0.003$ move. What stays
% here is what a reader cannot reconstruct from the pointer: that Mode~S identifies an intervention
% and not statistic disturbance alone, that the magnitude channel is open, and that a frozen control

\paragraph{What Mode~S does not hold fixed, and the control that closes it.} Mode~S identifies the intervention's effect at a fixed adversarial coefficient share, not statistic disturbance in isolation, so what it yields is a \emph{dissociation}, not proof that disturbance is inert. It pins the adversarial coefficients alone, so the dose still moves the aggregated update's magnitude and geometry, a third channel reading $\Delta$~agg.$={}0.892$ here. \textbf{Score-only Krum}, frozen before any score-only ASR existed, closes it by aggregating the \emph{untransformed} selected update at the same $\Delta$~dec.\ of $0.733$: $\Delta=-0.023$ against $-0.026$ uncontrolled on the same cell, so the claim is instrumented against both confounds, attenuation and magnitude (App.~\ref{app:score_only}, §\ref{app:decomposition}).

% Round 61, funding B3: 1045 chars to ~800. Three provenance recitals go to the appendix that already
% carries them verbatim: the frozen thresholds (confirm $>{+}0.178$, refute $<{+}0.150$) are :1501, the
% pooled $R^2{=}0.239$ is :1503 and also (L2) at :1980 and tab:tiers at :619, and the identity-rung ASR
% $0.443$ is :1503. The refutation, the discordance that makes it informative, and both qualifications
% stay here in the body; only the digits that a reader cannot use without the freeze document move.
\paragraph{A pre-registered replication that refutes the positive half.} \texttt{coord\_median} under the pixel backdoor is \emph{rank-discordant}: of the interpretable arms it has the smallest decision change ($0.482$) and the largest admission-level change ($\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.033$), so the two readings predict opposite rank positions for its rise, and the thresholds were frozen before its runs existed. We find $\Delta=+0.098$ at $n{=}5$, which \textbf{refutes the admission \emph{ordering}}: the arm with the largest admission-level change rose less than one with a smaller change, and the pooled decision reading does not predict it either.

\noindent What replicates is the negative on a coordinate-wise order statistic: that decision change costs no suppression by the frozen margin, at accuracy $0.698$. Equivalence here is to this cell's own identity rung and not to a low absolute ASR, and the rise is positive in $5/5$ seeds (post hoc): small and consistent, not zero (App.~\ref{app:targeted_full}).

% One paragraph, not two: the inline tabular that used to sit between them is gone, which left :205
% ending on a colon that pointed at a \noindent paragraph. And the reversal was being stated four
% times (here, twice below, and in Fig. 1's caption); it is now stated once, in the sentence the
% reviewer asked be made memorable.
\paragraph{The design choice, not the defense, sets the inferred direction.} On this identical cell (matched aggregate displacement, decision change and clean accuracy, Table~\ref{tab:comparability}), the outcome-gated ladder of §\ref{sec:dose_response}, whose adversary may attenuate, gives $\Delta$ASR${=}{-}0.273$ ($n{=}20$; paired $95\%$ interval $[-0.334,-0.213]$), where the Mode~S intervention above, which pins that adversary at $c{=}1$, gives $+0.125$ ($[+0.095,+0.155]$). The $n{=}5$ above is the same estimand at its frozen threshold's own $n$: the two differ in $n$, not in quantity. Collider bias is usually taught as attenuation; \textbf{here the confounded design does not lose power, it points the other way}, the one result to carry away: a criterion calibrated against that contrast would be calibrated against the confound.

\noindent The two designs are not the same intervention: $\Lambda_a$ moves in \emph{both}, $0.081$ against $0.033$ ($2.4\times$), so neither is a clean statistic-only intervention and no $p$-value attaches to their difference. What the pair does establish, \emph{within the rescaling class and defense family measured here} and by counterexample rather than by law, is that \textbf{downstream-statistic preservation is not sufficient to identify attack-suppression preservation} (Prop.~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability}, App.~\ref{app:proofs}; Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}), which is a statement about \emph{sufficiency} and not about a preserved statistic being uninformative.

\noindent \textbf{Seven cells, not one (App.~\ref{app:sixcell}).} One cell cannot separate a property of the design from one of this aggregator and attack, so we ran the contrast on seven. They disagree on five, across two attacks, three datasets and two CNN configurations, and \textbf{the reversal replicates}: the same cell on CIFAR-100 ($n{=}5$) reads $-0.213$ against $+0.071$, both intervals excluding zero. \texttt{coord\_median} under \emph{model scaling} disagrees too, so it is not specific to the pixel backdoor. A mechanism frozen for when they disagree is refuted out of sample and withdrawn, so §\ref{sec:protocol} says to intervene, not consult a rule.

\section{What the Boundary Licenses, and What We Found}
\label{sec:validation}

\emph{Does the pattern hold beyond the adjudicating arm, and what does a screen then buy?} The regime is CIFAR-10, a 4-layer CNN, $N{=}10$ clients, $f{=}0.2$ adversarial, with the attacks and seed counts of p\pageref{box:scope}; \emph{ASR} means \emph{max-committed ASR}, and the protocol is fixed in the supplement (§\ref{app:details}).

% Round 58: this blank line is LOAD-BEARING. A comment-only line comments out its own newline, so
% without it the paragraph above ran straight into the run-in head below and \noindent became a no-op:
% two paragraphs rendered as one, visible in the PDF and invisible to every LaTeX check.
% The four per-subsection \refs moved into §1's roadmap, so this paragraph keeps the one
% thing the roadmap does not carry, the TIER each answer sits in, and drops the navigation. Five
% cross-references in one paragraph became one range.
\noindent \textbf{Four questions, four kinds of answer}: \emph{algebra} before any run, one \emph{controlled intervention}, \emph{replication} on further datasets, and what the \emph{screen} costs in recall, banded into three unequal strengths by Table~\ref{tab:tiers}. The core evidence is a seed-matched intervention on one cell, not a mean comparison across cells, and any cross-arm ordering or exact admission-to-suppression magnitude sits in the weakest band with nothing resting on it.

% Round 61, page gate: all three of §6's subsection headings wrapped to a SECOND rendered line
% (pdftotext, pp8--9: "...AND WHERE ITS SIGN / DISAGREES"), so each cost one line of page 9 for no
% content. Shortened to fit one line each; every clause dropped from a heading is stated in the first
% sentence of the paragraph it heads, which is where a reader looks anyway. This is a deviation from
% this round's own "no retitling of any section" non-goal, taken deliberately and reported: the
% alternative was deleting three lines of prose, and a heading carries no claim.
\subsection{The confounded ladder, and where its sign disagrees}
\label{sec:dose_response}
\emph{What did the inadmissible design actually tell us?} Our earlier suites tried to validate ordering preservation by choosing a different downstream defense, the move Prop.~\ref{prop:identification} rules out. Varying only the \emph{dose} at fixed $d_2$ and attack ($\kappa{=}0$ bit-identical to $d_2$ standalone) refutes that suite's pooled prediction that disturbance predicts each arm's rise, and in three of four arms ASR \emph{falls} as the dose rises (one-sided $p{=}0.091$ against a frozen $p<0.05$; Table~\ref{tab:dose_outcome}). \textbf{The fault is ours and is now measured}: fixing $\mathrm{mean}(c){=}1$ leaves relative weights free, so a large spread mostly \emph{dilutes} whichever client carries the poison: attenuation, not statistic disturbance (§\ref{app:dose_caveats}, §\ref{app:delta_c}).

\subsection{The dissociation holds across four aggregators and two datasets}
\label{sec:targeted_rest}
% Round 61, page gate: the two numbers that used to stand here are a recital, not a home. App.
% score_only states the shrinkage verbatim and with more of it -- "+0.762 to +0.178", the one-sided
% Welch p, and the admission and decision moves the residue tracks -- so the pointer now goes to that
% paragraph rather than to its parent section. The self-correction itself, that our one confirming arm
% was mostly attenuation, stays in the body where a reader meets the ladder.
\emph{Is the dissociation one arm's accident?} Under the instrument, our one confirming arm was mostly attenuation: what remains of reputation's rise tracks a small \emph{admission} change, not the large decision change (App.~\ref{app:score_only}).

% Round 61: the margin clause that used to close this paragraph is gone, not lost. It said the same
% thing as :370's "the binding arm's $m^\ast$ is $0.034$", and this arm IS that binding arm, so the
% general statement covers it a fortiori; Table~\ref{tab:margin_ladder} prints the row and its caption
% carries the supplement pointer this sentence used to carry. One home, and it is the one that
% generalizes.
% Round 64, page-gate funding: this sentence carries the ResNet18 arm, and the two magnitudes trimmed
% out of it both keep a home. The EMNIST decision range's upper end ($0.714$) is :1801 verbatim, and
% the pooled EMNIST $n{=}5$ top-up ($-0.013$) is the "same arm, pooled" row of Table~\ref{tab:tost}
% at :2370 and the out-of-sample row at :1927. What stays is one decision fraction per arm, both
% pre-registered $\Delta$s at the $n$ they were frozen at, and the \texttt{cifar\_cnn} comparison.
\noindent \textbf{The Krum dissociation replicates on a second dataset and on a second architecture family}: pre-registered reruns on EMNIST-byclass/\texttt{simple\_cnn} and CIFAR-10/\texttt{resnet18} change the decision in $0.643$ and $0.556$ of rounds, the adversary never admitted, and both $n{=}3$ point estimates fall inside the frozen $\pm0.15$ margin, $-0.017$ $[-0.033,-0.001]$ and $-0.041$ $[-0.183,+0.101]$, beside \texttt{cifar\_cnn}'s $-0.026$ at $n{=}5$. Neither $95\%$ interval is a plain null: EMNIST's excludes zero, a real effect smaller than the margin, and \texttt{resnet18}'s is wider than the margin itself (App.~\ref{app:tost}, App.~\ref{app:targeted_full}).

\noindent \textbf{The dissociation also replicates outside the rescaling family the theory covers}: masking a random $0.8$ fraction of each \emph{benign} client's coordinates at fixed $L_2$ norm leaves adversarial updates exactly untouched, so nothing we prove predicts it: the decision changes in $0.467$ of rounds at admitted adversarial mass $0.000$ and suppression does not follow, neither pre-registered refuting branch firing (App.~\ref{app:factorial}). One mask is not the class of all non-rescalings.

\noindent \textbf{Two arms support the account rather than carry it.} \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every rung yet ASR falls $0.173$ at the first accuracy-gate-passing rung, from an identity $0.584$ at which it never suppresses (one-sided $p{=}0.044$, $n{=}8$, $5/8$ seeds), a pre-registered counterexample within the tested regime; and pinning payload share at fixed dispersion separates the theorem's two regimes, confirmed for \texttt{coord\_median} and refuted for reputation (App.~\ref{app:channel_table}, \ref{app:validation_full}, Table~\ref{tab:prediction_matrix}).

\subsection{What the screen cannot find, and what to run instead}
\label{sec:strategies}
% Round 58, reviewer item #9: they asked that the 2-of-2 precision result be made "almost invisible",
% and the reason is ours -- the closed-census positive moves LOW->HIGH as seeds go 3 to 8. Two
% paragraphs became one. Nothing left the paper: the whole frontier (42/12%/420, C2 alone 14/36%, the
% criterion 2 at 2/2 for 20+60=80 runs, and the reaches-FG->RFA column) is Table~\ref{app:frontier}'s
% own body and footnotes, which is where a reader who wants the rates now goes. The footnote there
% that used to say "that 80 is the figure §strategies quotes" was corrected in the same edit, because
% §strategies no longer quotes it.
\emph{What does the screen's own failure show?} \textbf{The screen fails exactly where Prop.~\ref{prop:emergent} says it must}: what it discards is FG$\to$RFA, the lowest-ASR composition in the menu, and \textbf{statistic-preservation screening, the rule this paper argues is not a causal surrogate, is the one that keeps it.} Against all nine variants an evaluator could run, the criterion sits at the high-precision, low-recall end (App.~\ref{app:frontier}, whose footnotes qualify both the rates and the C2 column). To find a menu's strongest composition, do not screen on inherited suppression at all. Two prospective tests price the menu and the criterion; the latter's four registered cells came out as predicted, and \textbf{that label does not survive more seeds}, where C1 fails and the criterion certifies nothing (§\ref{sec:prospective}, App.~\ref{app:census}).

% This paragraph said, at the end of §6, exactly what sec:validation's own tier paragraph already
% says (§2/§3 were swapped in Round 53, so name the label, not the number): same two
% pointers (tab:scope_levels, sec:limitations), same (L1)--(L6), same hedged-once claim. Its one
% non-redundant clause was grafted onto that paragraph rather than dropped; both labels and (L1)
% remain referenced from :71, :398 and :408, so nothing here was unique.
\section{Conclusion}

% Round 61, page gate: two clauses left this section and both have a home. The transportable rule's
% negative half ("rather than selecting the comparisons you make conditional on the outcome") is the
% next paragraph's second \emph{do not}, one paragraph below and in the imperative voice an evaluator
% acts on; what stays here is the positive rule §21.6 asked to be made actionable. And "they identify
% suppression preservation and not a screen for it" is the supplement's limitation 2 verbatim ("an
% instrument, not a screen ... not a (P4) screen"), Table~\ref{tab:tiers}'s oracle row, and the
% open-problem row of Table~\ref{tab:can_cannot}; the oracle fact itself stays, because it is what
% App.~\ref{app:conclusion} -- cited in this same sentence, and the section box:scope sends a reader to
% for it -- states as the open problem, and because the Ethics statement promises it at each use.
\textbf{Preserving a defense statistic does not suffice to identify preserved attack suppression}, and the reason is structural: an outcome-gated cross-defense comparison conditions on the outcome it seeks to explain, which no additional data repairs (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification}), and a screen of that shape also misses emergent suppression (Prop.~\ref{prop:emergent}). §\ref{sec:targeted}'s dissociations witness both directions. \emph{What transports is one rule, and it is not our screen}: to attribute an outcome to an upstream mechanism in a composed pipeline, hold the downstream mechanism and the threat fixed and intervene on the upstream component.

\textbf{What changes for an evaluator}, without using our criterion at all: \emph{do not} read a preserved statistic or an unchanged decision as evidence that suppression survived (Table~\ref{tab:channels}); \emph{do not} select the pairs you compare on the outcome you are about to attribute, the design that reversed our own sign; \emph{do} run §\ref{sec:protocol}'s four clauses, which measure the contribution admitted rather than the statistic feeding it, and report where the chain broke. Both instruments read adversary identity, leaving the open question (App.~\ref{app:conclusion}): \textbf{can admission (P4) be estimated without knowing which clients are malicious?}

\section*{Ethics statement}

This paper studies poisoning attacks in order to evaluate defenses against them, so its
subject matter is dual-use. Three things bound the risk. Every attack we run is already
published (model-scaling~\citep{bagdasaryan2020backdoor}, pixel-pattern
backdoors~\citep{chen2017targeted}, DBA~\citep{xie2019dba}) and we introduce no new attack
or new attack capability. All experiments are simulations on public benchmark datasets
(CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, EMNIST-byclass, UCI Spambase) with no human subjects, no personal data and no
deployed system involved. Our headline result is defensive and cautionary rather than
offensive: it tells practitioners that a preserved downstream statistic is \emph{not}
evidence that a composed defense stack still suppresses an attack, which removes a false
assurance rather than supplying an attacker with one. The instruments we build (Mode~S, the
payload-share instrument, score-only Krum) require knowing which clients are malicious and are therefore
measurement devices, not deployable defenses; we state this at each use so that no reader
mistakes them for a recommendation.

\section*{Reproducibility statement}

Every claim in the paper is tagged \emph{general}, \emph{formal} or \emph{empirical} at the
point it is made, and Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels} states the scope conditions on each.
For the formal claims, Propositions~\ref{prop:identification} and~\ref{prop:emergent} are
stated in full in the body and proved in the appendix, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} and Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} are stated and
proved in the appendix and referenced from the body. For the empirical
claims: each pre-registered arm has a decision rule git-committed before the corresponding
runs existed, with the commit hash recorded in the text and a runner that refuses to start
unless its own recorded commit matches; every table cell is emitted by a generator that
reads only frozen artifacts rather than being transcribed; sample sizes, seed ranges and
per-seed values are given for every arm, with per-seed detail in the appendix and the
supplement; and the figures are recomputed per seed by the same scripts. Where a number was revised we report
the revision and its cause rather than the corrected value alone, and where a derived
condition turned out to be empty on our data we report it as empty rather than omitting it
(§\ref{sec:restricted_theorem}). \textbf{No code or data package accompanies this
submission}, and throughout this paper ``the supplement'' means the companion PDF only. What
will be released on publication is specific rather than a general promise: every experiment
runner and measurement script, the \texttt{results/} JSON artifacts every reported number is
read from, the simulator itself, the generators that emit the figures, and the
$22$ pre-registration documents fixing the decision rules for every pre-registered arm, seed
top-up and audit, which is the count of \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_*.md}
and is checkable as one rather than taken on trust.
Everything this paper's tables and figures are computed from runs from the repository root on
Python~3 with \texttt{numpy}, \texttt{scipy}, \texttt{matplotlib}, \texttt{torch} and
\texttt{torchvision}. The analysis entry points behind them are \textbf{idempotent}:
they read the frozen artifacts and rewrite them byte-identically, so every number we report is
a deterministic function of a frozen artifact rather than of a re-run, and no table cell can
drift between builds. What the paper itself provides is the pre-registration commit hashes,
the frozen decision rules they fix, and per-seed values for every arm, which is what
is needed to check the reported numbers against a reimplementation.

\section*{AI use statement}

In this work, we used generative AI tools as a writing and analysis assistant: for prose
editing and revision of the manuscript, for \LaTeX{} restructuring, and for writing
verification and analysis scripts that check the manuscript against the frozen result
artifacts. We did not use generative AI tools to generate research ideas, to formulate the
hypotheses, to design the experiments or pre-registrations, or to produce any experimental
result, and no claim, number, figure or table in this paper was authored by an AI tool. We
have reviewed all AI-assisted work: AI-assisted prose was checked against the underlying
artifacts sentence by sentence, and AI-written analysis scripts were run against frozen
inputs whose outputs are reproducible independently of the tool. We take responsibility for
the final content of this work, including text, claims and artifacts produced with the aid
of generative AI.
