\textbf{PASS precision: $2$ of the $2$ selected pairs are low-ASR.} That is $100\%$ as a descriptive statistic, and the positive class is two cases, so it resolves the direction and not the rate. Under the tightened per-attack C1 (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}) the certified in-distribution set is 2 pairs, fg$\to$cm ($0.107$) and rep$\to$cm ($0.463$ at $n{=}30$), both correct; fg$\to$rfa ($0.093$) is no longer certified and is reported below as a false negative of our own criterion. This is the practitioner-relevant result: the criterion's positive predictions are reliable, at the cost of 40\% recall. Overall development-set accuracy: \textbf{16/18 = 89\%}. Two misses, both in the HIGH direction:

\textbf{One certified pair is measured to the threshold, and a pre-registered top-up did not resolve it.} Rep$\to$CM's LOW label rests on its pixel arm, whose $n{=}5$ interval $[0.231,\,0.616]$ crossed $0.5$ with one seed at $0.665$, so we pre-registered seeds $47$--$71$ (\texttt{de77a7f}: seed list, unchanged $0.5$ rule and the three admissible outcomes all fixed before any run) and took that cell to $n{=}30$, the same power as the pair the criterion rejects. It is \textbf{still unresolved}: mean $0.463$, $95\%$ interval $[0.412,\,0.514]$, with $10$ of the $30$ seeds above the threshold. Six times the seeds narrowed the half-width $3.8\times$ ($0.192 \to 0.051$) and moved the mean \emph{up} from $0.423$, toward the threshold rather than away, so the cell is near-threshold rather than merely noisy. The LOW label holds by the frozen mean rule, the pre-registration forbids adding seeds once the outcome is seen, and we therefore stop here and report it unresolved: the certified in-distribution set is $2/2$ correct on the mean, and only fg$\to$cm is certified \emph{and} resolved below threshold. The frozen seed reproduced bit-for-bit before the top-up ran ($0.43178$ at seed $42$), so the $25$ new seeds extend the original five rather than replacing them; the scaling arm stays at $n{=}5$ ($0.020$), where it cannot set the max. Clean accuracy is $0.755$ over the $30$ runs, and $0.50$, $0.68$, $0.76$ on the three cells, all well clear of the collapse regime.

\paragraph{Two boundary conditions, stated alongside the primary result.} Both are developed in full later, but neither should be met late. \textbf{(i) Extreme heterogeneity breaks two of the three PASS pairs.} At $\alpha{=}0.1$, fg$\to$rfa reaches $0.569$ and rep$\to$cm $0.548$; only fg$\to$cm survives, at $0.433$. What makes this more than a negative datapoint is that the failure appears in the theorem's own quantity: RFA's separation margin $m := \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/[2(R'_B+\delta')]$, measured on the transformed points, collapses from an all-round mean of $3.24$ to $1.01$ (exactly the $m \to 1$ boundary at which Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound degrades to $40\%$ adversarial mass), so a practitioner running the 5--10 round pilot would measure $m \approx 1$ and decline to deploy. We emphasise that this is a post-hoc measurement of a known failure, not a pre-registered prediction of it; full sweep and caveats (bimodality at $n{=}3$, accuracy collapse to $0.20$--$0.48$) in §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E). \textbf{(ii) Mechanism preservation is not adaptive-attack robustness.} A criterion-aware adversary solving FoolsGold's and RFA's constraints jointly (decorrelated coordinate blocks to evade the similarity test, $\epsilon$-ball projection to stay inside the geometric median's radius) raises fg$\to$rfa from $0.045$ (seed-matched, and on the pixel arm the attack is built from) to $0.167$ within our evaluated composition-aware attack family (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}). Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} bounds what the preserved mechanism admits, not what an adversary reshaping its update to satisfy both constraints at once can do, and the $3.7\times$ increase is the size of that gap on our flagship pair.

\textbf{Held-out validation (pre-registered): 22/24 = 92\%} (\emph{Screening}: an evaluation ordering, and by Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} not an identification of the mechanism's contribution)\textbf{.} Table~\ref{tab:composability_heldout} reports the 24 pre-registered pairs. Combined across both waves: \textbf{38/42 = 90.5\%}. \textbf{Read against its base rate that figure is nearly uninformative, which is why it appears here, where it is computed, and in no summarizing position.} Only 5 of the 42 pairs are low-ASR (the $12\%$ base rate of §\ref{sec:condition_scoring}), so the constant predictor that calls every pair HIGH already scores $37/42 = 88.1\%$; $90.5\%$ is one pair better than that, and the tally is carried by correct negatives. The informative quantities are precision $2/2$ and recall $40\%$ (§\ref{sec:condition_scoring}). The two held-out misses are both LOW-predicted pairs that were flagged as \emph{speculative} in the pre-registration (neither is a clean C1$\wedge$C2$\wedge$C3 PASS):

Only the two LOW predictions are shown individually; the 22 HIGH predictions (all correct) are summarized.}

\textbf{Rep$\to$tm: composition dominates despite C1-FAIL.} Reputation$\to$TrimmedMean is correctly classified as C1-FAIL (pixel ASR 0.679 $> 0.5$), yet it is the paper's main composition result. The resolution: the criterion predicts \emph{max-committed ASR}, while the deployment-relevant metric is \emph{oracle ASR} (0.316). The oracle cannot exploit the pixel gap because scaling (which reputation \emph{does} suppress) dominates its utility. The criterion thus correctly identifies that rep$\to$tm does not suppress every committed attack, while the game-theoretic analysis shows it is strategically dominant.

\textbf{Tightening C1 costs us our own flagship, and reveals a synergy case.} Under the per-attack quantifier in C1, \textbf{fg$\to$rfa no longer certifies}: neither FoolsGold ($0.732$) nor RFA ($0.845$) suppresses the pixel backdoor individually, and FoolsGold's $0.200$ against model-scaling is hollow because it comes with $10\%$ clean accuracy. The composition nonetheless reaches $0.093$. This is genuine \emph{synergy} (suppression neither constituent possesses) and Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full} proves that \emph{no} inherited-suppression screen can certify it, so this is a scope boundary rather than a tuning failure. We therefore report our lowest-ASR composition as a false negative of our own criterion. The certified in-distribution set is fg$\to$cm ($0.107$) and rep$\to$cm ($0.463$), both correct: precision $2/2$, recall $2/5 = 40\%$.

\textbf{A \emph{directional} C1 would empty that certified set, and we report the price rather than pay it.} The natural next tightening is to replace C1's ``some constituent suppresses $a$'' with the directional ``\emph{$d_2$} suppresses $a$'', on the ground that C0 forces $d_2$ anyway. \textbf{C0 does not force it.} C0 is existential over attacks while C1 is universal over them, so Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} pins $d_2$ only on the witnessing attack $a^*$; off $a^*$ the constituent holding that arm may be $d_1$, and the two definitions accordingly disagree on $4$ of the $42$ pairs. On all four the downstream defense is \texttt{coord\_median}, whose standalone model-scaling ASR is $0.519$ and so above threshold, while the upstream \texttt{foolsgold} ($0.200$) or \texttt{reputation} ($0.017$) is what holds that arm; every C1 input here is the $n{=}5$ single-defense baseline on seeds $42$--$46$. \textbf{Both certified pairs are of exactly that kind}, so a directional C1 selects nothing in distribution and leaves precision \emph{undefined} instead of $2/2$. \textbf{What the substitution correctly points at is real, and it is a quantifier and not a direction}: on the model-scaling arm of these two pairs the suppression is inherited from an \emph{upstream} defense, which nothing downstream can undo, so the signal-preservation argument contributes nothing to that arm even though the arm counts toward the max-committed label. The criterion and Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} are therefore aligned \emph{per attack} and not per pair. We keep C1 and disclose the asymmetry; the census is emitted by \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak analyze\_condition\_ablation.py}.

\subsection{A prospective test on a never-run defense menu}
\label{sec:prospective}
\noindent \emph{Does the criterion predict on a menu it has never seen?}
\textbf{Result: 13/20, and the shortfall is in our experimental design.} At the same $0.5$ threshold: TP${}=8$, FP${}=0$, TN${}=5$, FN${}=7$ (Table~\ref{tab:prospective}). \textbf{The suite carried the sanity gate our out-of-sample battery lacked}, pre-registered with the labels: a predicted-FAIL pair counts only if the attack clears ASR $0.5$ against FedAvg, which both committed attacks do ($0.667$ scaling, $0.768$ pixel), so a pair cannot be scored correct because the attack failed on its own. Every predicted-LOW pair came out LOW, $8/8$, and that figure is far weaker than it looks, because \textbf{FLTrust suppresses both committed attacks on its own} (max-committed ASR $0.048$ at $0.575$ clean accuracy), the first defense in our menu that does. All 13 pairs containing FLTrust land in $[0.041, 0.078]$, within $0.030$ of FLTrust alone, in either slot. The two slots fail for different reasons, and the distinction matters:

\textbf{The two slots fail differently, and the difference is the finding.} \emph{(i) FLTrust as $d_1$ (7 pairs, 2/7).} $d_1$ alone already suppresses, so the composition inherits that suppression whatever $d_2$ does; all five predicted-HIGH misses have real C2/C3 failures, but destroying $d_2$'s mechanism is costless when $d_2$ was never load-bearing. This is C0, invisible on the original 42 pairs because no defense there suppresses alone (the minimum single-defense max-committed ASR is CoordMedian's $0.519$, and the lowest ASR among all 39 non-PASS pairs is $0.433$), so we count these 7 pairs out of scope \emph{retrospectively}: a weaker claim than a pre-registered exclusion. \emph{(ii) FLTrust as $d_2$ (6 pairs, 6/6), but the prediction is nearly an identity.} FLTrust gates on the scale-invariant $\cos(u_i, g_{\mathrm{srv}})$ and then renormalizes every update to $\|g_{\mathrm{srv}}\|$, discarding magnitude entirely, so a positive per-client rescaling is \emph{exactly} cancelled: four of the six pairs are bit-identical to FLTrust alone or differ only within seed noise. The single genuine deviation is FG$\to$FLTrust ($0.064$), the one $d_1$ producing exact \emph{zeros} rather than positive rescalings and hence a Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} interaction. This arm is a consistency check between theory and implementation, \textbf{not} a falsifiable test: every $d_1$ in our menu is a positive rescaling and Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) permits no other outcome. \emph{(iii) Neither defense is FLTrust (7 pairs, 5/7)}: the only arm that could have surprised us. Five predicted-HIGH pairs are correct ($0.870$--$0.943$), though only $2$ of the arm's $7$ pairs compose two real mechanisms once the pass-through and MultiKrum degeneracies are accounted for (below); both misses are diagnosable and neither is a mechanism error: fg$\to$krum measures $0.078$ at $9.9\%$ clean accuracy (the collapsed-model artifact, not suppression), and krum$\to$cm measures $0.450$ against a $0.5$ threshold as a degenerate pair reducing to CoordMedian alone, whose own baseline is $0.519$. The arm contains \emph{zero} predicted-LOW pairs, so it bears on specificity only. Per-pair numbers for all three arms are in the supplement (§\ref{app:prospective_arms}).

\textbf{What the suite does and does not establish.} Counting only in-scope pairs gives $11/13$; the arm that carried real risk of falsification gives $5/7$ with both misses explained. But it is not the prospective positive the framework needs, because \textbf{it contains none}: by (i) and (ii) the 8 predicted-LOW pairs are 6 near-identities plus 2 out-of-scope inheritances.

\noindent \textbf{We withdraw the three matched contrasts we previously drew here}, which we had read, $d_1$ fixed while $d_2$ moved from FLTrust to a Euclidean rule, as a $19$--$20\times$ ASR gap turning on exactly the property Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} identifies. Two ran against MultiKrum, which at $K{=}5$ selects \emph{all five} participants and is bit-identical to FedAvg ($1.9\times10^{-6}$ per aggregation; a genuine selector again at $K{=}10$), so they carried no downstream defense at all. Substituting Krum does not repair them, and that is the finding: all three Krum arms take their max-committed ASR from the pixel backdoor, which Krum \emph{fails to suppress standalone} ($0.583$), so the gap measures C1 failure and not disturbed invariance, and nc$\to$krum is bit-identical to Krum alone on all three seeds because under that attack no client exceeds $\tau{=}5$ and $d_1$ is the exact identity. Counting the pass-through $d_1$ rows too, $8$ of the $20$ rows reduce to a single defense rather than the $5$ labelled DEGEN at freeze, and arm (iii) composes two real mechanisms in $2$ pairs, not $7$ (App.~\ref{app:mkrum}). The frozen labels and the $13/20$ score stand; what we retract is our reading of them. The confound is accordingly worse than we reported: in this menu the invariant-signal $d_2$ is also the only $d_2$ that suppresses alone, so C1 and C2 co-vary. Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} explains why no other choice of defense would have helped: in scope, C0$\wedge$C1 \emph{force} $d_2$ to suppress $a^*$ alone, so the confounding is structural. Breaking it requires holding the suppression principle and standalone effectiveness fixed while varying only the invariance class, which is the design of §\ref{sec:metric_swap}; that section's $\Delta$ analysis is the only contrast in either paper holding C1 exactly fixed, and it is where our C2 evidence now rests.

\subsection{Breaking the confound: a matched metric-swap ablation}
\label{sec:metric_swap}

\noindent \emph{Can the confound be broken by swapping the metric instead of the defense?}
Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} says mechanism--effectiveness confounding cannot be escaped by picking a different defense off a menu: in scope, C0$\wedge$C1 force the invariant-signal $d_2$ to be one that suppresses $a^*$ alone. So we break the confound by construction instead, holding the suppression \emph{principle} fixed and varying only the invariance class of the statistic $d_2$ reads.

\textbf{The design's premise was refuted by its own baselines.} Matching failed in both arms, for two independent reasons, and because the C1 inputs are measured before the freeze we learned this before the suite ran: the only reason it can be reported as a finding rather than a post-hoc excuse. \texttt{cos\_reputation} does not merely weaken relative to its Euclidean original, it collapses, from Reputation's $0.017$ on model-scaling to $\mathbf{0.982}$. \texttt{cos\_krum} reaches $0.078$ on model-scaling, which passes C1's numeric threshold, but at $\mathbf{0.146}$ clean accuracy, the hollow-suppression artifact already documented for FoolsGold ($0.200$ at $0.10$) and fg$\to$krum ($0.078$ at $0.099$), so C1 fails under the accuracy gate we apply everywhere else. The mechanism is visible in the baselines rather than asserted: undefended FedAvg under model-scaling is itself at $0.100$ accuracy, so Krum's $0.577$ is \emph{restoring} utility the attack destroyed and \texttt{cos\_krum}'s $0.146$ fails to. \textbf{Magnitude information is what lets this defense family handle a magnitude attack}: a direction-only mirror of a Euclidean scorer cannot see a scaling attack at all, so removing the magnitude dependence removes the suppression with it: exactly the coupling the pre-registered contingency anticipated, stated there as the possibility that ``invariance and standalone effectiveness may be inherently coupled'' for this family. On the magnitude-neutral pixel backdoor the mirror does survive ($0.300$ at $0.598$), but there its control Krum fails C1 ($0.583$), so those arms are unmatched in the opposite direction and the pixel cells cannot carry the test either.

The consequence is that \textbf{the confound re-materialized inside an experiment built specifically to break it}, which we report as the round's methodological finding rather than as a failed run. Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} establishes that mechanism--effectiveness confounding is structural in scope; this is what trying to escape it looks like when the mechanism being varied is the mechanism the defense relies on. Two responses were available and we took neither. We did not switch to a pixel-only protocol, which would have made \texttt{cos\_krum} pass C1 and manufactured two predicted-LOW cells: choosing the attack after seeing the C1 inputs is attack-shopping regardless of the resulting labels. We did not adjust the accuracy gate to admit $0.146$, which would have produced a predicted-LOW pair by weakening the very criterion that catches hollow suppression elsewhere. The pre-registration's second non-negotiable (``no switching of committed attack, threshold, seed count, or accuracy gate'') forecloses both, and either would have bought a positive test at the cost of the property that makes the test worth running.

\textbf{Outcome: 8/8 correct, and zero positive tests.} Because C1 binds for all eight pairs (on model-scaling through the two baseline failures above, on the pixel backdoor by design) every frozen label was HIGH, so the suite bears on specificity only, as a consequence of inputs measured before the freeze rather than a choice made afterwards. We ran all 8 pairs $\times$ 2 attacks $\times$ 3 seeds anyway, because a suite whose labels are all one class is still falsifiable in the direction that matters: a single LOW cell would have been a false positive against a criterion claiming $100\%$ precision. All eight came out HIGH ($8/8$ correct, TP $=0$, FP $=0$, TN $=8$, FN $=0$, max-committed ASR spanning $0.508$--$0.935$) with labels frozen in \texttt{pre\_registration\_metric\_swap.md} at commit \texttt{356897b}, timestamped before \texttt{results/metric\_swap/} existed and reproduced verbatim in the runner's \texttt{PAIRS} list so scoring cannot drift. We state the limitation in the same breath as the result: the suite contains \textbf{no falsifiable prospective positive test}, and $8/8$ is not validation of the framework's predictive claim: eight correct negatives on pairs our own conditions could only have called HIGH is weak evidence, and it supports a claim about specificity, not prediction.

One prediction in the suite was genuinely two-sided, and it is the round's only prospective positive result. \texttt{cos\_krum}'s C1 verdict on model-scaling turns entirely on whether the accuracy gate is applied ($0.078$ passes the numeric threshold; $0.146$ accuracy fails the gate), so rather than choose a reading afterwards we froze \emph{both}, with the resolution rule fixed in advance: accuracy-gated $\Rightarrow$ HIGH, numeric-only $\Rightarrow$ LOW, and a LOW outcome at accuracy $\ge 0.35$ would have recorded our two primary labels as misses. Both pairs came out \textbf{HIGH} (NormClip$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} $0.508$, RFA$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} $0.566$), refuting the secondary reading and vindicating the accuracy gate against a pre-registered alternative specification of itself on data neither of us had seen. The weaker half is flagged in the supplement (§\ref{app:metric_swap_detail}): the NormClip arm sits barely above threshold and it is the RFA arm that carries the conclusion.

\textbf{A post-hoc contrast that is not confounded.} The cross-pair scoring above is confounded in exactly the way Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} predicts, but the frozen suite happens to contain a comparison that is not, and it is the strongest C2 evidence in either paper. Compare a composition against \emph{the same} $d_2$'s own standalone ASR on the same attack, under two different upstream transforms. C1 is then identical by construction (same downstream defense, same attack, same measured standalone effectiveness on both sides), so the residual $\Delta = \mathrm{ASR}(d_1{\to}d_2, a) - \mathrm{ASR}(d_2, a)$ cannot be attributed to $d_2$ being a stronger or weaker defense. What does vary is how much the upstream transform actually disturbs $d_2$'s statistic, and that quantity was measured on the mechanism itself (\texttt{results/cos\_invariance\_check.json}) rather than inferred from any ASR. The frozen label scoring is fixed at $n{=}3$ and must not move, so we topped the six powered cells up to $n{=}5$ in a separate directory, with the tested direction fixed in the top-up script before the new seeds ran (\texttt{results/metric\_swap\_topup/}). Because $\Delta$ is a difference, the subtrahend moves with it: each topped-up cell's standalone is recomputed at the \emph{same} five seeds, from the dose ladder's $\kappa{=}0$ rung, which is that $d_2$ alone under the identical configuration (§\ref{app:ladder}) and agrees with the published standalone baselines bit-for-bit on every seed they share (largest deviation $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ in ASR and accuracy across 16 seed-level comparisons), so it is the same quantity carried to five seeds and not a second measurement of it. Scoring a five-seed composition against the three-seed baseline would hide a mixed-$n$ comparison inside the subtrahend, where it is invisible, and on one cell below it would invert the conclusion. For Krum under model-scaling (standalone $0.062$ at $0.565$ accuracy over those five seeds; $0.061$ at $0.577$ on the frozen three): NormClip changes Krum's selection in $0/9$ rounds and the composition reaches $0.062$ ($\Delta = +0.001$, per-seed $0.120 / 0.018 / 0.047 / 0.058 / 0.068$); RFA changes it in $6/9$ rounds and the composition reaches $0.779$ ($\Delta = +0.717$, per-seed $0.974 / 0.116 / 0.982 / 0.904 / 0.916$): a gap of $+0.716$ between two compositions sharing a downstream defense, an attack, and a baseline, and at $n{=}5$ it is statistically resolved (Welch $t = 4.28$, two-sided $p = 0.012$; paired $t = 4.60$, $p = 0.010$; Mann--Whitney one-sided $p = 0.008$), which it was not at $n{=}3$.

The pattern holds across the suite once cells without power are excluded: $\Delta$ is observable only where standalone $d_2$ genuinely suppresses, since a baseline already above $0.5$ shows $\Delta \approx 0$ by ceiling effect whatever the transform did. Restricting to standalone ASR $< 0.5$ at accuracy $\ge 0.35$ leaves six cells, and at $n{=}5$ measured disturbance still splits them perfectly: the three undisturbed cells ($0/9$) give $\Delta \in [-0.044, +0.001]$, the three disturbed ones ($>0/9$) give $\Delta \in [+0.717, +0.878]$: a separation of $+0.716$ with no overlap, wider than the $+0.629$ the frozen $n{=}3$ scoring gives. That separation is the \emph{same} $+0.716$ as the Krum contrast above and not independent evidence on top of it: both extremes are those two Krum cells, which share a standalone, so the width of the split is algebraically that contrast's gap. The other four cells extend the two halves outward rather than the distance between them. Two of the three undisturbed cells are \texttt{cos\_krum} on the pixel backdoor, where the mirror suppresses standalone ($0.300$ at $0.598$ accuracy on the frozen three seeds, $0.490$ at $0.607$ on all five, since two of the added seeds are high-ASR) and carries that suppression through under \emph{both} upstreams. Under NormClip it carries through \emph{exactly}: NormClip never binds on this attack (max client norm $3.04$ against $\tau = 5$, $0/9$ rounds), so the composition is bit-identical to the baseline at all five seeds and $\Delta = +0.000$ is an identity rather than a measurement; under RFA the composition is $0.447$ against that $0.490$, i.e.\ $\Delta = -0.044$. Kept against the frozen three-seed baseline these same rows would instead read $+0.190$ and $+0.147$, and the natural reading of \emph{that}, the magnitude channel of Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} moving $\Delta$ where the selection is exactly invariant, would credit an effect to a transform that provably did nothing: the movement is entirely the baseline's seed set. So this contrast has no power on the magnitude channel in either direction, because its one exactly-invariant downstream sits under an upstream that never rescales; measuring that channel requires moving the dial on purpose, which is what the ladder's arm 3 (§\ref{app:ladder}) does prospectively. The ten excluded cells are listed rather than dropped silently in the supplement (§\ref{app:metric_swap_detail}).

The suite contains a second such contrast, on Reputation under model-scaling (standalone $0.017$ at $0.777$ accuracy), and the two are informative in opposite ways. NormClip disturbs Reputation's weight ordering in $3/9$ rounds and the composition reaches $0.806$; RFA disturbs it in $9/9$ and the composition reaches $0.895$. The gap is only $+0.089$ (both arms are far above threshold, because Reputation's ordering is disturbed under \emph{both} upstreams) but within-arm variance is small, all five seeds move in the predicted direction (per-seed deltas $+0.149 / +0.089 / +0.030 / +0.061 / +0.115$), and it is resolved at both $n{=}3$ and $n{=}5$ (Welch $t = 3.06$, two-sided $p = 0.018$; paired $t = 4.31$, $p = 0.013$). So the two contrasts trade off exactly as the disturbance measurements imply: Krum has the large effect because one of its upstreams leaves it untouched, and Reputation was resolvable even at $n{=}3$ because neither upstream does.

\textbf{This is post-hoc, and we do not present it otherwise.} It was not pre-registered, it re-uses a suite frozen for a different purpose, the two extra seeds per cell were added \emph{after} seeing the $n{=}3$ result, and the six-cell split is a description of that suite rather than a test on new data. The top-up could have cut in either direction and we report what it did: it resolved the Krum gap (Welch $p = 0.16$ at $n{=}3$, where per-seed paired deltas $+0.854 / +0.097 / +0.935$ left one seed not separating, to $p = 0.012$ at $n{=}5$) and left the invariant control at $\Delta \approx 0$ ($+0.000$ under NormClip, $-0.044$ under RFA), the added seeds raising its baseline and its compositions together. What the analysis does have, which neither the FLTrust contrasts of §\ref{sec:prospective} nor the cross-pair scoring has, is C1 held exactly fixed and a disturbance measurement taken on the statistic rather than read back from the outcome. We offer it as the cleanest available piece of C2 evidence and as the shape a properly prospective version of this test would take: fix a downstream defense that suppresses the attack, then vary the upstream along measured disturbance rather than along defense identity. Table~\ref{tab:can_cannot} sets out, claim by claim, what this design can and cannot conclude.

\subsection{An application: screening as evaluation prioritization}
\label{sec:condition_scoring}
\label{app:prioritization}

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{\textbf{What this design can and cannot conclude.} The middle column is about
\emph{identifiability}, not statistical power: a ``no'' marked \emph{structural} cannot be repaired by
more seeds, defenses or attacks (Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full}). Every entry is scoped to the
measured setting of §\ref{sec:composability_validation}--§\ref{app:targeted_full}.}
\label{tab:can_cannot}
\centering\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.40\linewidth}p{0.16\linewidth}p{0.38\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{question} & \textbf{identifiable?} & \textbf{answer, and its evidence} \\
\midrule
Does disturbing $d_2$'s statistic change its \emph{decision}? & measured & \textbf{Yes}: Krum's selection flips in up to $0.80$ of rounds \\
Does it change the \emph{admitted adversarial mass}? & by construction & \textbf{No}: unchanged in every one of the $60$ measured rounds \\
Does it change \emph{suppression}? & instrumented & \textbf{No}: $|\Delta|{=}0.026$, inside the frozen margin (Prop.~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability}(a)) \\
Does \emph{adversary-correlated} reweighting change suppression? & instrumented & \textbf{Yes}, non-monotonically: single-peaked (Mode~A) \\
% Round 63: the n is printed here because the frozen refutation stays at its own n=5 while the
% comparability cell's controlled leg moves to n=20 (pre_registration_reversal_seed_topup.md,
% Amendment 2, Class B item 2; the site list is fixed in Amendment 3). Same estimand, two reporting
% roles, so a site that quotes it without an n can be read as the topped-up one.
Does admitted mass \emph{order} outcomes across defenses? & pre-registered & \textbf{No, refuted}: $+0.098$ ($n{=}5$) against a frozen ${+}0.150$ \\
Is (P4) \emph{sufficient} for (P5)? & control arm & \textbf{No}: \texttt{cos\_krum} admits $0.000$ while ASR falls \\
Can (P4) be screened \emph{without} adversary identity? & \textbf{no, open} & Both instruments are oracles: the stated open problem \\
Does C1 identify C2's contribution? & \textbf{no, structural} & Empty cell by the gate, for every menu (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification_full}) \\
Does any of this certify \emph{deployed} security? & \textbf{no, scope} & Committed attack only; one adaptive test breaks it \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\textbf{What follows is a consequence of the identification boundary, not a claim the paper argues for.} Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} says a criterion gated on the outcome it predicts cannot be validated by cross-defense contrast; what such a criterion can still do is \emph{order an evaluation budget}, and that is the only use we claim for it. Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full} bounds what it can find. Read the numbers below as a measurement of a prioritization heuristic's cost, not as validation of the framework.

\textbf{Does every condition earn its place? C3 does not: reported as a finding.} Scoring all 42 pairs on C1, C2 and C3 \emph{independently} rather than recording only the first failure: against a $12\%$ base rate of low-ASR pairs, C1 alone selects 4 at $50\%$ precision, C2 alone 14 at $36\%$, and C1$\wedge$C2 selects 2 at $100\%$. \textbf{The surviving C2 failures are exactly the non-invariant signal class}: after the Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} reclassification every one of them is a $\cdot{\to}$reputation pair (nc$\to$rep $0.819$, rfa$\to$rep $0.882$, fg$\to$rep $0.798$), consensus distance being the signal a positive rescaling does not preserve, while all three non-degenerate $\cdot{\to}$FoolsGold pairs are C1 failures instead. \textbf{C3 has no demonstrated incremental predictive value in this study}: alone it selects 35 pairs at $9\%$ precision, below the base rate, and it never binds on a pair C1$\wedge$C2 already admits. So \textbf{we demote C3 from a gate to a margin diagnostic and recommend C1$\wedge$C2 as the criterion}, retaining C3 on theoretical grounds only: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} shows margin compression is a real failure mode, and the $\alpha{=}0.1$ regime below is precisely a margin collapse. \textbf{The demotion changes no number}, C1$\wedge$C2 selects the same two pairs at the same precision (recomputed by \texttt{analyze\_screening\_cost.py}), which is why we report it as a finding about the criterion rather than a revision of results. \emph{The pre-registered PASS/FAIL labels and the $16/18$ and $22/24$ tallies were frozen with C3 in the gate and are not relabelled}; this ablation is post-hoc and labelled as such, and the recommendation is for use going forward.


\paragraph{If the goal is to \emph{find} emergent compositions, drop C1: a recommendation we price but do not validate.} Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full} says an inherited-suppression gate cannot certify a composition whose suppression neither constituent possesses, so an evaluator whose object is discovery rather than prioritization should not run C1 at all, and this is a consequence of the proposition rather than a finding of ours. Two designs follow, and §\ref{app:frontier} prices both on our own menu. \textbf{Sample the ungated menu} when the budget allows: $42$ pairs at $12\%$ precision reaches every low-ASR pair by construction, and it is the only design with no structural blind spot. \textbf{Otherwise gate on the mechanism condition alone}, C2 without C1: it admits $14$ of the $42$ at $36\%$ precision, a third of the ungated cost, and it reaches FoolsGold$\to$RFA where every C1-bearing variant misses it. \textbf{The caveat is ours and it is not small}: C2-alone's recall on this menu is $100\%$, but $11$ of those $14$ admissions rest on a per-pair call made in sample rather than on algebra, and all $5$ low-ASR pairs are among the $11$ (§\ref{app:frontier}), so that recall is a development-set number and we offer the design as a recommendation rather than as a validated procedure. Finding emergence efficiently is a search problem, and nothing here solves it: what the propositions establish is which designs cannot solve it.

\subsection{The screening frontier: precision, recall and evaluation cost}
\label{app:frontier}

\noindent \emph{What does the screen cost, and what does it cost to find out?}
\textbf{$40\%$ recall is an operating point, not a verdict on the conditions.} Scoring C1, C2 and C3
independently over the $42$ pairs traces a frontier, and the criterion the paper reports is its
high-precision end. Emitted by \texttt{experiments/build\_screening\_frontier.py}, which joins the two
existing generators and introduces no number of its own.

\noindent \textbf{Neither screen is noise, and neither is a predictor.} Both C2 alone and C1$\wedge$C2 beat a size-matched random draw from the same $42$ pairs ($P \le 0.005$ at $10^5$ resamples against the menu's $12\%$ base rate, seeded \texttt{rng(42)}; emitted by \texttt{experiments/blind\_selection\_analysis.py}). Beating a random draw is the whole of what that establishes: it separates the screens from chance and not from each other, and it says nothing about either one's ability to predict suppression.

\noindent \textbf{The blind spot is attributable to exactly one condition, and the theory named it
before the ablation was run.} Every C1-bearing variant misses FoolsGold$\to$RFA and every C1-free
variant reaches it: its row is C1 \emph{false}, C2 \emph{true}, C3 \emph{true}, max-committed ASR
$0.045$. C1 is the inherited-suppression gate, and Prop.~\ref{prop:emergent_full} proves no gate of that
form can certify an emergent composition, so the miss is a \emph{predicted} property of one condition
rather than an unexplained failure, and it is tradeable: C2 alone recovers every low-ASR pair in the
menu at $36\%$ precision. \textbf{We report the high-precision end because false positives deploy an
insecure stack}, not because the conditions cannot be operated at higher recall.

\noindent \textbf{The C2 column is not all one kind of evidence, and the high-recall claim depends on
the weaker kind.} We describe C2 as read off the two defenses' definitions, and for two of its four
branches that is literally what happens: a $d_2$ reading a cosine is exactly invariant under any
positive per-client rescaling (Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}(a)) and a $d_2$ reading a consensus
distance, a residual magnitude or a norm is not (Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}(c)). The remaining branch
is \emph{conditional}: \texttt{coord\_median} and \texttt{trimmed\_mean} preserve a coordinate ordering
only under a separation condition, and Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2) gives \texttt{rfa} an
analogous one, so \texttt{analyze\_condition\_ablation.py} marks all three \texttt{None} and
\texttt{c2\_holds} defers to a \emph{per-pair call}, the stored category. Nothing in the C2 column
distinguishes a verdict reached by algebra from one reached
by that call, so we census it (\texttt{c2\_provenance}, same generator): of the $14$ pairs C2 admits,
\textbf{$3$ rest on algebra and $11$ on the per-pair call}, the $11$ being every
\texttt{coord\_median}, \texttt{trimmed\_mean} and \texttt{rfa} row. \textbf{And all $5$ low-ASR pairs
are in the $11$}: $0$ of $5$ are algebraically settled. Hence the $\ddagger$ row above. \textbf{Worse
for us, all $11$ are development-set pairs}, so those calls were made \emph{in sample}, while the
criterion was being written; they are not pre-registered, and the generator derives the split from
which results artifact holds each pair rather than taking our word for it. \textbf{Two
consequences we accept rather than repair.} First, C2 alone as we score it \emph{is not a pure
statistic-preservation screen} for those three $d_2$ classes, so the body's contrast between it and
C1$\wedge$C2 is a contrast between two screens that both borrow from the same in-sample call, not
between algebra and measurement. Second, \emph{within this menu} the region where our own invariance
result decides the question contains no low-ASR pair at all, so the algebraically settled sub-region
cannot discriminate any strategy from any other \emph{here}, and the $100\%$ recall of C2 alone is
carried entirely by in-sample judgement. \textbf{The second consequence is the one we then removed},
by enlarging the algebraic region until it closed rather than by relabelling this one: the $3$ pairs
of the $\ddagger$ row are one column of a $3\times3$ census, and completing it prospectively puts a
low-ASR pair inside the algebraic region and separates the two strategies more sharply there than
anywhere in this table (§\ref{app:census}). The \emph{first} consequence stands and needs an
invariance theorem for the conditional classes, which we do not have; what neither needs is a
relabelling, and no number in this appendix or the body changes. This is (L5) in concrete form: the
conditions are not always readable off a $(d_1,d_2)$ pair.

\noindent \textbf{What the screen saves, in runs rather than in pairs.}
\texttt{experiments/analyze\_screening\_cost.py}: unscreened, the $42$ pairs under $2$ committed
attacks at $5$ seeds are $420$ runs; the reported criterion evaluates $16$ cells, $80$ runs, of which
$60$ are the standalone measurements C1 needs. That is $340$ runs avoided, \textbf{$81\%$ of runs}. We
give the run-level figure rather than the pair-level $95.2\%$, which overstates the saving by ignoring
C1's own standalone cost. \textbf{The unit is runs, and the hours behind them are now measured
rather than assumed}: the runners print per-run seconds into their logs, and the six the script reads ship with the code,
so the figure is checkable; over the $228$ logged
CIFAR-10 pair-evaluation runs the median is $11.1$ min (mean $13.1$, IQR $10.7$--$12.5$), so the
$340$ avoided runs are $63$ GPU-hours on the one machine, or $74$ at the mean. \emph{Correcting our
own caveat:} through the previous version this paragraph said no per-run timing existed anywhere in
the results and the artifact assumed $11.0$ min/run. The runner logs did carry it; the assumption
happens to have been accurate to $0.14$ min, and we report the measurement in its place because a
figure that can be measured should not be assumed. EMNIST-byclass arms run $52$ min/run and are
excluded, so this accounting is CIFAR-10 only.

\subsection{A closed census of the algebraically settled region, pre-registered}
\label{app:census}

\noindent \emph{What happens where the algebra settles preservation exactly, with no cells unspent?}
\textbf{The $\ddagger$ row above is one column of a $3\times3$ box, and the two thirds of that box
which had never been run are what this wave measured.} The region where mechanism preservation is
settled on \emph{both} sides is small and enumerable: $d_1$ emitting strictly positive per-client
coefficients $T(u_i) = c_iu_i$, so Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) applies, against a $d_2$ reading an
exactly scale-invariant statistic. Over this project's defenses and the metric-swap mirrors that
region is $\{\texttt{norm\_clip},\texttt{rfa},\texttt{reputation}\} \times
\{\texttt{foolsgold},\texttt{cos\_krum},\texttt{cos\_reputation}\}$, exactly $9$ ordered pairs, of
which $7$ were already measured at the identical protocol and $2$ were unspent. Enumeration is what
turned a planned $20$-pair sample into a census: \textbf{$118$ ordered pairs of this project are
already spent}, and once the admissibility constraints (a non-degenerate $d_1$, and C0) are imposed
only $6$ candidates remain anywhere. \textbf{A census needs no sampling argument, and an all-negative
census is as informative as a positive one}, which is why this design was worth $24$ runs and
$4.87$ h. Pre-registered at \texttt{96f8856} with per-pair C0--C3 verdicts, a frozen
scoring rule and a two-sided resolution table
(\texttt{experiments/pre\_registration\_wave3\_emergent.md}); scored by
\texttt{experiments/analyze\_wave3\_emergent.py}, which refuses to run against an uncommitted prereg.

\begin{center}\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{6pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
\toprule
$d_1 \downarrow$ \quad $d_2 \rightarrow$ & \texttt{foolsgold} & \texttt{cos\_krum} & \texttt{cos\_reputation} \\
\midrule
\texttt{norm\_clip}  & $0.887$ HIGH & $0.508$ HIGH$^\dagger$ & $0.935$ HIGH$^\dagger$ \\
\texttt{rfa}         & $0.914$ HIGH & $0.566$ HIGH           & $0.861$ HIGH \\
\texttt{reputation}  & $0.807$ HIGH & $\mathbf{0.263}$ \textbf{LOW}$^\ast$ & $0.787$ HIGH$^\ast$ \\
\bottomrule
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\footnotesize Max-committed mean ASR over seeds $42,43,44$; LOW iff $< 0.5$.} \\
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\footnotesize $\ast$ the two unspent cells, labels frozen at \texttt{96f8856}
before the output directory existed.} \\
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\footnotesize $\dagger$ \texttt{norm\_clip}'s clip is inactive on the pixel arm
(max update norm $3.043 < \tau{=}5$), so those} \\
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\footnotesize \emph{pixel} cells are bit-identical to $d_2$ alone; both verdicts
are carried by the scaling arm, where it is not.} \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent \textbf{\texttt{foolsgold} is excluded from the $d_1$ column by measurement, and that
measurement changed the design before any prediction was frozen.} It was going to supply two more
C1-fail $\wedge$ C2-true pairs. But \texttt{foolsgold} normalizes with
\texttt{weights - weights.min()}, forcing at least one coefficient to \emph{exactly} zero every
round, which puts it outside Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}(a)'s strict $c_i > 0$ hypothesis and under
Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} instead. Measured through the shipped transform over $3$ seeds
$\times\ 3$ rounds: $2/5$ exact zeros every round, and both downstream cosine statistics moved in
$9$ of $9$ rounds. Synthetically at $1000$ trials the effect is entirely due to the zero: strictly
positive coefficients change \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection $0/1000$ times, one exact zero changes it
$471/1000$. \texttt{reputation} by contrast emits $0/5$ zeros every round at a positive-part ratio up
to $18\,839.8$, and under that same extreme rescaling in the same rounds the two cosine statistics
moved $0/9$ while non-invariant \texttt{krum}'s selection moved $8/9$ and \texttt{reputation}'s own
ordering $9/9$: \textbf{a contrast between statistics on identical inputs, not between conditions.}
So the two \texttt{foolsgold}$\to\texttt{cos\_}\ast$ pairs were entered as \emph{measured-C2-fail
controls} rather than as members of the census. \textbf{Had C2 been assumed from the definitions
instead of measured, this wave would have reported two mislabelled pairs}
(\texttt{experiments/verify\_wave3\_invariance.py}, \texttt{results/wave3\_invariance\_check.json}).

\begin{center}\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llccl@{}}
\toprule
\# & pair & cell & pred. & measured \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{reputation}$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} & certified (C1$\wedge$C2) & LOW & $\mathbf{0.263}$ \textbf{LOW} \checkmark \\
2 & \texttt{reputation}$\to$\texttt{cos\_reputation} & C1-fail $\wedge$ C2-true & HIGH & $0.787$ HIGH \checkmark \\
3 & \texttt{foolsgold}$\to$\texttt{cos\_krum} & measured-C2-fail control & HIGH & HIGH$^\S$ (raw $0.086$) \checkmark \\

and not a suppressed one.} \\

\end{center}

\noindent \textbf{$4/4$ as registered, including the pair we flagged \texttt{uncertain}, and the
near-identity check came back clean on every arm} ($0$ of $3$ seeds identical to $d_2$ alone on all
$8$ arms; max per-seed differences $0.073$ to $1.000$), so these are compositions and not $d_2$ in
disguise. Pair 1 was the live risk and the prereg said so: its LOW rests on two \emph{different}
attacks being suppressed by two \emph{different} constituents, \texttt{reputation} holding
model-scaling ($0.017$ at accuracy $0.777$) and \texttt{cos\_krum} the pixel backdoor ($0.300$ at
$0.598$), and on the scaling arm the certification and the two nearest measured analogues
($\texttt{norm\_clip}\to\texttt{cos\_krum}$ $0.508$, $\texttt{rfa}\to\texttt{cos\_krum}$ $0.566$)
pointed \emph{opposite} ways. It came out LOW on both arms ($0.064$ scaling, $0.263$ pixel).
\textbf{So the one low-ASR pair in the closed census is a pair neither constituent suppresses alone},
and it is the pair C1$\wedge$C2 selects.

\noindent \textbf{Both strategies priced over the same closed census, which costs no further runs
because C1 is defined on standalone numbers that already exist.}

\begin{center}\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{6pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrr@{}}
\toprule
strategy over the $9$-cell census & selected & precision & recall & runs \\
\midrule
A: statistic preservation (C2 alone) & 9 & $11\%$ & $100\%$ & 90 \\
B: the criterion (C1 $\wedge$ C2)    & 1 & $\mathbf{100\%}$ & $\mathbf{100\%}$ & \textbf{10} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent In this region C2 alone is not a screen at all: it admits everything, because everything
here satisfies it by construction. \textbf{That inverts the body's trade}, where B bought precision
by giving up recall; here, at the frozen seed count, B dominates A on precision, recall and cost at
once, and disclosure \emph{(v)} below is the qualification that phrase needs. It is also the
answer to the objection of §\ref{app:frontier} that the algebraically settled sub-region cannot
discriminate strategies: it could not, at $3$ pairs; at $9$ it discriminates them completely.

\noindent \textbf{Five disclosures, none of which the numbers above carry on their face.}
\emph{(i) B's $1/1$ is a gated reading.} C1 requires suppression at mean clean accuracy
$\ge 0.35$, the gate applied throughout §\ref{sec:metric_swap}, and it flips exactly two census cells:
\texttt{cos\_krum} alone reaches ASR $0.078$ on model-scaling but at accuracy $0.146$, barely above
CIFAR-10's $0.10$ chance, so it is a destroyed model rather than a defended one. Read at the bare
$0.5$ threshold, C1 additionally admits $\texttt{norm\_clip}\to\texttt{cos\_krum}$ and
$\texttt{rfa}\to\texttt{cos\_krum}$, both HIGH, and B selects $3$ for $33\%$ precision. The gate is
the paper's pre-existing convention rather than a knob turned for this table, and
\texttt{analyze\_wave3\_emergent.py} emits both readings. \emph{(ii) The one positive is $n{=}3$ and
wide}: pair 1's pixel arm is $0.000/0.107/0.681$ per seed, so one of three seeds sits above the
threshold and the LOW verdict is the frozen mean rule applied to a spread that a fourth seed could
move. We do not add seeds after seeing this. \emph{(iii) The controls are weaker evidence than
intended.} Every \texttt{foolsgold}-as-$d_1$ scaling arm sits at chance accuracy ($0.100$), and pair
3 is HIGH only through the accuracy gate, its raw ASR being $0.086$, and its scaling arm tripped the
frozen ``ASR identically $0.0$ across all $3$ seeds'' flag, which on a model at $0.100$ accuracy means
the attack had nothing left to succeed against; so pairs 3 and 4 establish that
the C2 probe is not inert, which is their stated role, but they are not independent confirmations of
a HIGH prediction. \emph{(iv) $7$ of the $9$ cells are retrospective.} Only the two \texttt{reputation}
cells were blind; the other seven were read from frozen artifacts, with each transcription in the
prereg checked against its source by the analyzer. So the census's \emph{closure} is prospective at
the margin, not throughout, and pair 2's scaling arm tripped the second of the wave's two frozen sanity
flags (clean accuracy $0.790$ against the FedAvg reference $0.667$). Both flags are reported, neither adjusted.
\emph{(v) The C1 input that certifies pair 1 is the lowest-seed reading of a high-variance cell.} C1
holds for pair 1 on the pixel arm because \texttt{cos\_krum} alone measures $0.300$ there, and that is
the mean over the three frozen seeds $42$--$44$ ($0.003/0.269/0.630$). The identical cell, same
protocol and identity upstream, is measured twice more in this paper at larger seed counts, and the
cell's mean climbs monotonically with the count: $0.490$ at $n{=}5$ (Table~\ref{tab:dose_outcome},
arm 3's $\kappa{=}0$ rung, which adds $0.864/0.686$) and $0.584$ at $n{=}8$
(Table~\ref{tab:targeted}, which adds $0.578/0.845/0.795$), the last being the reading
§\ref{app:targeted_full} states as \texttt{cos\_krum} never suppressing. Six of the eight seeds are
above $0.5$ and the frozen three are the two lowest plus the fourth-lowest. So B's precision $1/1$
and recall $1/1$ here are contingent on the frozen seed count as well as on the accuracy gate of
\emph{(i)}, and we state the contingency at the resolution the numbers support rather than rounding
it against ourselves: $0.490$ is still \emph{below} the threshold, by $0.010$, so C1 survives at
$n{=}5$; at $n{=}8$ C1 fails for pair 1, B certifies nothing in this census, and its one low-ASR
pair becomes another false negative of the criterion rather than its one hit. We report this instead of rescoring,
because the pre-registration fixes the seed set at three and forbids adding seeds afterwards
(\texttt{pre\_registration\_wave3\_emergent.md}, §5). What the disclosure costs is the claim that B
\emph{dominates} A on this region, which holds at the frozen seeds and is not established beyond them.

\subsection{Out-of-Sample PASS Validation}
\label{sec:oos_pass}

The development and held-out validation above tests predictions from the \emph{same} attack families (model\_scaling, backdoor\_pixel) and scale ($N{=}10$, cifar\_cnn) used to design the criterion. To confirm that PASS predictions generalize we pre-registered three out-of-sample tests (commit \texttt{ca627e5}, before any of them ran); all held, the accounting is in the summary below, and per-cell values for every test are in the supplement (§\ref{app:oos_detail}). We state plainly what this is and is not: it is evidence that the conditions keep identifying compositions which survive a change of attack family, scale and architecture; it is \emph{not} an out-of-sample classification result, because every prediction in it is positive and the matching FAIL controls proved uninformative. Negative-direction evidence rests entirely on the in-distribution tiers.

\textbf{(E) Heterogeneity: where the criterion breaks.} Earlier heterogeneity sweeps in this work covered \emph{single defenses} only; no composition had been run at any $\alpha \neq 0.5$. We ran all three PASS pairs at $\alpha \in \{0.1, 1.0, 10.0\}$ (3 seeds, both committed attacks, 50 rounds); the $\alpha{=}0.5$ column is each pair's headline value at its own seed count ($n{=}30$ for fg$\to$rfa and rep$\to$cm, $n{=}5$ for fg$\to$cm) rather than a 3-seed rerun. Reporting max-committed ASR / clean accuracy:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
\textbf{Composition} & $\alpha{=}0.1$ & $\alpha{=}0.5$ & $\alpha{=}1.0$ & $\alpha{=}10.0$ \\
\midrule
fg$\to$rfa & \textbf{0.569} / 0.20 & 0.093 / 0.50 & 0.051 / 0.57 & 0.063 / 0.62 \\
fg$\to$cm & 0.433 / 0.40 & 0.107 / 0.68 & 0.133 / 0.74 & 0.129 / 0.78 \\
rep$\to$cm & \textbf{0.548} / 0.48 & 0.463 / 0.76 & 0.377 / 0.79 & 0.326 / 0.81 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The criterion transfers cleanly across $\alpha \in \{0.5, 1.0, 10.0\}$, including the near-IID regime where FoolsGold might plausibly founder on legitimately similar benign updates; it does not, and $\alpha{=}10$ is indistinguishable from $\alpha{=}0.5$. \textbf{Under extreme heterogeneity ($\alpha{=}0.1$), two of the three PASS compositions cross the threshold} (fg$\to$rfa $0.569$, rep$\to$cm $0.548$); only fg$\to$cm survives, at $0.433$. Rep$\to$cm's $\alpha{=}0.5$ baseline is itself at the threshold ($0.463$ at $n{=}30$), so its crossing is the smaller move of the two. Two caveats cut in opposite directions: fg$\to$rfa at $\alpha{=}0.1$ is strongly bimodal across seeds ($0.065$--$0.923$), so at $n{=}3$ the direction is clear but the magnitude is not; and clean accuracy collapses to $0.20$--$0.48$, so this regime stresses utility as much as security. Crucially Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} supplies a \textbf{diagnostic quantity that retrospectively explains this boundary} rather than predicting it: re-measuring on the transformed points at $\alpha{=}0.1$, RFA's separation margin $m := \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/[2(R'_B+\delta')]$ collapses from an all-round mean of $3.24$ (met in 68\% of rounds) to $1.01$ (met in 20\%). Over the payload-carrying rounds alone (the ones Figure~\ref{fig:mechanism} plots) the same collapse reads $4.71 \pm 1.51$ ($n{=}17$) to $2.06 \pm 2.35$ ($n{=}12$), with the per-round minimum falling $2.82 \to 0.00$; the all-round means are lower because they average in the Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} rounds, where FoolsGold zeroes every adversary and $m$ is recorded as $0$. Either way the collapse is measured, and the all-round figure is the one that reaches the vacuous limit. This sits exactly at the $m \to 1$ boundary where Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound degrades to $40\%$ adversarial mass: extreme heterogeneity inflates the benign radius $R'_B$ until the benign cluster is no longer separated from the adversarial points, which is precisely the precondition the theorem requires. A practitioner running the 5--10 round pilot would measure $m \approx 1$ and decline to deploy, so the criterion is mechanistically diagnostic at this boundary, though we emphasise this is a post-hoc measurement of a known failure, not a pre-registered prediction of it.

The one axis not validated with composition experiments is \emph{dataset}: every composition in this paper is CIFAR-10, and the two arms measured on another dataset are single-arm intervention ladders rather than composition suites, EMNIST-byclass/\texttt{simple\_cnn} in §\ref{app:targeted_full} and CIFAR-100/\texttt{cifar\_cnn} (100-class output) in §\ref{app:sixcell}. The conditions are dataset-agnostic in mechanism, but the downstream aggregator's decision boundary may shift with the number of classes, and the CIFAR-100 ladder is direct evidence that it can: the same cell that reverses sign on CIFAR-10 reverses sign there too, at a different magnitude (L1).


\section{The intervention evidence: the confounded ladder, and the instrument it motivated}
\label{app:ladder}

\textbf{The controlled upstream transform.} We introduce a synthetic $d_1$ whose only parameter is the \emph{dispersion} of a positive per-client rescaling. For the $K$ participants of a round, at position $j$ in a $(\text{seed},\text{round})$-keyed pseudorandom permutation,
\begin{equation}
c_j \;\propto\; \exp\!\Big(\kappa \cdot \Big(\tfrac{2j}{K-1} - 1\Big)\Big), \qquad \text{normalized so } \textstyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_j c_j = 1 .
\label{eq:dose}
\end{equation}\begin{table}[h]
\caption{The four arms of the dose--response ladder, and the frozen predictions. The statistic each $d_2$ reads is, in order, summed pairwise Euclidean distance, distance to the client median, pairwise cosine, and coordinate ordering. \emph{Standalone} is $d_2$'s own ASR (clean accuracy) on that attack, the C1 input, held fixed within every arm. \emph{Measured disturbance} is the fraction of live rounds on which the rung changes that arm's decision (for \texttt{coord\_median}, the mean fraction of \emph{coordinates} whose median-attaining client changes) measured before the freeze on the same raw updates, with no ASR computed (\texttt{results/dose\_disturbance.json}). Shapes and decision rules are frozen at commit \texttt{e711a95}.}
\label{tab:dose_design}
\centering\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{cllcccccl}
\toprule
& \textbf{arm} ($d_2$ / attack) & \textbf{class} & \textbf{standalone} & \multicolumn{4}{c}{\textbf{measured disturbance}} & \textbf{predicted} \\
& & (Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}) & ASR (acc) & $\kappa{=}0$ & $0.5$ & $1.0$ & $2.0$ & \textbf{shape in $\kappa$} \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{krum} / scaling & (c) not inv. & $0.061$ ($0.577$) & $0.00$ & $0.60$ & $0.73$ & $\mathbf{0.87}$ & monotone rise \\
2 & \texttt{reputation} / scaling & (c) not inv. & $0.017$ ($0.777$) & $0.00$ & $0.87$ & $0.87$ & $\mathbf{0.93}$ & monotone rise \\
3 & \texttt{cos\_krum} / pixel & \textbf{(a) exact} & $0.300$ ($0.598$) & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & $\mathbf{0.00}$ & \textbf{FLAT} (control) \\
4 & \texttt{coord\_median} / pixel & (b) cond.\ inv. & $0.443$ ($0.767$) & $0.00$ & $0.25$ & $0.38$ & $\mathbf{0.49}$ & rise, shallower \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}


\textbf{The abscissa is measured, not nominal.} What matters to C2 is not $\rho$ but what the rescaling does to the statistic $d_2$ actually reads, so before freezing any prediction we pushed live FL updates through the shipped transform and recorded each arm's decision at every $\kappa$ on the \emph{same} raw updates, computing no ASR. That establishes three things before any outcome exists (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}): \emph{there is a dose}: disturbance is exactly $0$ at the identity rung and rises monotonically with $\kappa$ in every non-invariant arm; \emph{the class-(a) invariance claim holds in the shipped code}: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection changes in $\mathbf{0}$ of $120$ cells at $\rho$ up to $54.6$; and \emph{class (b) is graded rather than binary}, at roughly half the class-(c) disturbance ($0.487$ vs.\ $0.867$/$0.933$ at $\kappa{=}2.0$), which is what arm 4's prediction turns on.

\textbf{Frozen predictions.} The \emph{primary} test is pooled: across all 16 $(\text{arm},\kappa)$ cells, the rise in ASR over that arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung is predicted to be positively rank-correlated with the measured disturbance rate (Spearman, one-sided, $\alpha = 0.05$). Its predictor is a \emph{measured property of the statistic}, not the identity of the defense and not its standalone effectiveness, both held fixed within every arm. The \emph{secondary} predictions are the per-arm shapes of Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}, tested by a one-sided Jonckheere--Terpstra trend test across the four ordered rungs at $n{=}5$, with arm 3's ``flat'' scored as an \emph{equivalence} claim at a $0.15$ margin fixed and justified in advance (under a quarter of the $+0.629$ within-defense gap for Krum (the $n{=}3$ value on record at the freeze; the later top-up puts it at $+0.716$, so the margin is if anything conservative) and $0.300+0.15 = 0.450 < 0.5$, so ``within the margin'' and ``still suppressing'' coincide for this arm) and arm 4's class-(b) signature being the \emph{shallower slope}.

\textbf{One prediction we deliberately do not make.} Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)'s ordering-survival condition $\rho < S$ would give arm 4 a crossing rung fixed by a measured $S$, but \textbf{no frozen prediction here uses $S$}: we measured it and it is \textbf{not a stable per-attack constant}, ranging $0.00$--$12.59$ on model-scaling (median $0.57$) and $0.61$--$1.63$ on the pixel backdoor (median $0.92$) across the 12 rounds that sampled an adversary, so the $S = 10$ used illustratively in §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem} is a stipulation rather than a measurement. This is a limitation of our own theorem that the dose-response measurement surfaced, reported as one (L5) rather than quietly left unchecked.

\subsection{Outcome of the confounded design: one arm confirmed, two refuted, one indeterminate, and the pooled test fails}
\label{sec:dose_outcome}

\noindent \emph{What did the outcome-gated ladder actually return, arm by arm?}
All 80 runs completed (16.5 h wall clock). Table~\ref{tab:dose_outcome} is the whole ladder; Figure~\ref{fig:dose_ladder} plots it on the nominal dose axis against the frozen shapes and Figure~\ref{fig:dose_measured} on the independently measured disturbance axis with the real-transform anchors, scored by \texttt{analyze\_dose\_response.py} against the rules frozen at \texttt{e711a95}, every number recomputed per seed from \texttt{results/dose\_response/}. The \textbf{primary} pooled test does not confirm: across the 16 cells, the rise in ASR over each arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung correlates with the measured disturbance at Spearman $\rho_s = +0.351$, one-sided $p = 0.091$, so the frozen criterion ($\rho_s > 0$ at $p < 0.05$) is not met and \textbf{we report the primary prediction as refuted}. Of the four frozen shapes one is confirmed, two are refuted, and one is indeterminate; \textbf{in three of the four arms ASR \emph{falls} as the dose rises}. This is the round's headline result and it is a negative one. It is also the outcome the pre-registration anticipated and named in advance: ``pooled correlation absent while individual arms move: the mechanism is defense-specific and measured disturbance is not a transferable predictor.''

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{The dose--response ladder as measured, $n{=}5$ per cell, per-attack mean ASR with mean clean accuracy in parentheses. \emph{Predicted} is the shape frozen at commit \texttt{e711a95} before any run; $p_{\uparrow}$ is the one-sided Jonckheere--Terpstra probability for an \emph{increasing} trend across the four ordered rungs, which is the only direction any frozen rule tests. Verdicts apply the frozen criteria unchanged (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design} and the supplement §\ref{app:dose_protocol}); an arm satisfying neither its confirmation nor its refutation criterion is reported as \emph{indeterminate} rather than scored in our favour. $^{\dagger}$ below the $0.35$ clean-accuracy gate: a collapsed model, not suppression. Student-t 95\% intervals and per-seed values are in the supplement (§\ref{app:dose_detail}).}
\label{tab:dose_outcome}
\centering\scriptsize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2pt}
\begin{tabular}{cllccccrl}
\toprule
& \textbf{arm} ($d_2$ / attack) & \textbf{predicted} & \multicolumn{4}{c}{\textbf{mean ASR (clean accuracy)}} & \textbf{JT} & \textbf{verdict} \\
& & \textbf{shape} & $\kappa{=}0$ & $0.5$ & $1.0$ & $2.0$ & $p_{\uparrow}$ & \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{krum} / scaling & monotone rise & $0.062$ ($0.57$) & $0.011$ ($0.65$) & $0.014$ ($0.68$) & $0.054$ ($0.62$) & $0.63$ & \textbf{refuted} \\
2 & \texttt{reputation} / scaling & monotone rise & $0.017$ ($0.78$) & $0.022$ ($0.78$) & $0.487$ ($0.77$) & $\mathbf{0.779}$ ($0.70$) & $1.7{\times}10^{-6}$ & \textbf{confirmed} \\
3 & \texttt{cos\_krum} / pixel & \textbf{FLAT} (control) & $0.490$ ($0.61$) & $0.450$ ($0.57$) & $0.153$ ($0.47$) & $0.086$ ($0.22$)$^{\dagger}$ & $0.999$ & indeterminate \\
4 & \texttt{coord\_median} / pixel & rise, shallower & $0.443$ ($0.77$) & $0.428$ ($0.76$) & $0.327$ ($0.75$) & $0.170$ ($0.69$) & $0.998$ & \textbf{refuted} \\

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.82\linewidth]{figures/dose_ladder.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{The ladder and its frozen predictions.} The 16 cells on the nominal dose axis $\rho = e^{2\kappa}$ ($n{=}5$ per point, Student-t 95\% intervals), coloured by Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} class. Of the two class-(c) arms whose statistics the framework's fragility claim is stated over, only reputation rises; ASR \emph{falls} with the dose in three of the four arms, against the predicted rises. Hollow markers fail the $0.35$ clean-accuracy gate and are uninterpretable. Drawn by \texttt{paper/figures/dose\_response\_figure.py}, which recomputes every value from \texttt{results/} and imports the arm construction from the scoring script so the two cannot diverge.}
\label{fig:dose_ladder}
\end{figure}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.86\linewidth]{figures/dose_measured.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{Why a transform's flip rate is not a sufficient statistic for what it does.} ASR against the \emph{independently measured} disturbance of each arm's own statistic ($n{=}5$, Student-t 95\% intervals), the abscissa fixed before the freeze. Stars and crosses are the two \emph{real} upstream transforms measured on these same $(d_2, \text{attack})$ cells (NormClip, RFA), placed at their own measured disturbance rate: at matched disturbance they sit $+0.77$ and $+0.79$ ASR \emph{above} the synthetic curve, while their realized weight ratios (mean $3.61$ and $11.93$, maximum $4.90$ and $17.38$) fall \emph{inside} the ladder's span. The class-(a) arm's four rungs coincide at disturbance exactly $0$ ($0/120$ selections changed, maximum relative score drift $1.1\times10^{-4}$) and its ASR falls anyway: Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s magnitude channel, not C2 disturbance.}
\label{fig:dose_measured}
\end{figure}

\textbf{Arm 2 confirms: the ladder yields one prospective confirmation, while the pooled prediction is rejected.} Reputation under model-scaling rises monotonically from $0.017$ at the identity rung to $0.487$ at $\rho = 7.39$ and $0.779$ at $\rho = 54.6$ (Jonckheere--Terpstra $z = +4.64$, one-sided $p = 1.7\times10^{-6}$; permutation $p < 5\times10^{-5}$), while clean accuracy moves only $0.777 \to 0.701$, so what is lost is suppression, not the model. Same $d_2$, same attack, same standalone baseline at every rung: nothing about effectiveness varies, and $55\times$ dispersion in a \emph{positive per-client rescaling} (a transform the criterion treats as benign for invariant statistics) carries a defense from $0.017$ across the $0.5$ threshold. Two qualifications keep this in proportion. It validates the \emph{destruction} half of C2's mechanism (a non-invariant statistic, actually disturbed, loses suppression); the \emph{preservation} half (invariance $\Rightarrow$ suppression survives) is arm 3, which came out indeterminate. And even within this arm the measured disturbance is not what orders the curve: it saturates at $0.867$ by $\kappa{=}0.5$, where ASR is still $0.022$, and the jump to $0.487$ happens at the next rung at the same measured disturbance, so the \emph{dose} moves ASR here while the flip rate does not.

\textbf{Ecological validity: the real transforms do not lie on the synthetic curve.} Pre-registered caveat (iv) required this check, and it fails in an informative way. RFA$\to$Krum sits at ASR $0.779$ with measured disturbance $0.667$, where the synthetic ladder's nearest rung ($0.600$) gives $0.011$: a residual of $+0.767$. NormClip$\to$Reputation sits at $0.806$ with disturbance $0.333$, against $0.017$ at the identity rung: $+0.789$. Two anchors do land: RFA$\to$Reputation, $0.895$ at disturbance $1.000$ against $0.779$ at $0.933$ ($+0.116$), and both \texttt{cos\_krum} anchors, which coincide with its curve ($+0.000$ and $-0.044$) because that statistic is invariant under all three transforms. This is not a range mismatch: read off the same live rounds, NormClip realizes $\rho$ of mean $3.61$ (max $4.90$) and RFA mean $11.93$ (max $17.38$), both inside the ladder's $\{1.00, 2.72, 7.39, 54.60\}$ span. The conclusion is about our abscissa: \textbf{the rate at which a transform flips a defense's decision is not a sufficient statistic for what that transform does to the defense}: two transforms at matched flip rate differ by up to $0.77$ in ASR. \textbf{And the axis they \emph{are} unmatched on is now measured, not conjectured.} Adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c := \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}) separates the transforms carrying those anchors from the ladder: RFA reaches $\Delta_c = -1.21$ and NormClip $-0.72$, against $-0.34$ at the ladder's most dispersed rung: $2$--$3.6\times$ more extremely targeted at $\rho$ that is $3.7$ and $13\times$ \emph{smaller}, so \textbf{matched on the statistic, badly unmatched on the targeting, and $\rho$ does not order $\Delta_c$}. \textbf{This rescues no predictor}: $\Delta_c$'s magnitude does not order suppression either; rfa$\to$krum still fails at $0.779$ at $\Delta_c = -1.21$, while \texttt{reputation}, targeted harder still on both attacks, has rep$\to$coord\_median suppressing, and FoolsGold's $\Delta_c$ is \emph{positive} ($+0.663$). These figures are \textbf{post-hoc and exploratory}, computed with no ASR and rescoring nothing, and are tabulated in the supplement (§\ref{app:delta_c}). Arm 4 has no real-transform anchor at all, since \texttt{coord\_median} was not on the metric-swap menu, so its ecological validity is untested.

\textbf{What we now claim less of.} C2's causal content is established prospectively for one statistic (distance to the client median) and refuted for another (summed pairwise distance) at $\rho$ up to $54.6$; the pooled claim that measured disturbance predicts loss of suppression \emph{across} defenses is refuted, and we no longer make it. Within-defense disturbance remains the only design the testability boundary admits, and §\ref{app:boundary}'s general statement stands untouched by this outcome; it is a statement about admissible designs, and this is the admissible design, run and reported as it landed. What changes is the empirical status of C2: one confirmed arm, not a validated screening mechanism.

\subsection{The targeted intervention: isolating C2 from the attenuation channel}
\label{app:targeted_full}
\label{app:channel_table}

\noindent \emph{With the adversary's share pinned, how much of each arm's rise survives?}
\textbf{Reading \texttt{cos\_krum}'s entry in Table~\ref{tab:channels}.} Its $-0.425$ is the full-ladder endpoint, which lies below our accuracy floor; \textbf{the witness we rest on is instead its first, gate-passing rung} ($0.173$, §\ref{sec:targeted_rest}). The rank discordance between $\Delta\Lambda_a$ and $\Delta$ASR across the two moving arms is §\ref{sec:targeted_rest}'s refutation of our own predicted ordering. This section gives the emitter and the per-artifact provenance for every cell of that table.

\textbf{Round counts and per-row provenance, relocated here from the table's caption.} \emph{Admission} here is Table~\ref{tab:channels}'s $\Delta$~adm., the \emph{support} of the admitted adversarial mass, which that caption keeps distinct from the mass itself ($\Delta\Lambda_a$); it never moves in any measured round of any arm: $60$ rounds per CIFAR-10 \texttt{cifar\_cnn} arm, $56$ on the EMNIST-byclass replication and $72$ on the ResNet18 replication, $368$ in all, including the $48$ per arm where reputation and \texttt{coord\_median} carry adversarial mass at baseline, so the constancy is not an artifact of arms that never admit an adversary at all. The decision/admission dissociation is accordingly not one aggregator's property: it holds for a selector, a cosine-invariant selector, a coordinate-wise order statistic and a weighted averager, which is what the table's \emph{Kind} column records. Row~5 replicates row~1 on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN, with its channels measured \emph{before} its ASR existed; row~6 is the pre-registered score-only control, row~1 with the magnitude channel closed, so its decision, admission and influence columns are row~1's own; row~7 replicates row~1 with \emph{architecture} varying alone, its channels also measured before its ASR existed (§\ref{app:resnet18_dose}). \textbf{Row~7 is printed last rather than beside row~5, which is where it belongs by subject}: four sites in this paper index the score-only control as row~6, and the emitter orders the rows so that adding a seventh cannot silently falsify them. The table is \emph{not} an out-of-sample test. \textbf{Row~5 is the frozen $n{=}3$ in every column, its $\Delta$~ASR included}, because its three channel columns were measured at that seed count and were not re-measured: the same cell's $\Delta$~ASR at $n{=}5$ is $-0.013$, reported with its provenance in disclosure~\emph{(i)} below, and substituting it here would put one $n{=}5$ cell in a row of $n{=}3$ ones.

\label{app:score_only}

\textbf{What Mode~S does to the ladder's confirmation.} Reputation was the ladder's one confirmed arm (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}); Mode~S shows most of that confirmation was attenuation. With the adversary's share held fixed, reputation's rise at $\rho{=}54.6$ shrinks from the ladder's $+0.762$ to $+0.178$ (one-sided Welch $t{=}{-}5.22$, $p{=}0.0017$ that it is smaller), and what remains tracks a small \emph{admission} change ($0.000 \to 0.020$) rather than the large decision change ($0.000 \to 1.000$), so H-admission is confirmed here too (Table~\ref{tab:targeted}). The confound is thus \emph{explained}, not explained away: a genuine, much smaller, admission-driven effect survives it. The class-(a) control cuts the other way and refutes us: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every rung (decision and admission both $0.000$), yet ASR is $0.584$ at the identity (it never suppresses) and \emph{falls} $0.425$ across the ladder ($p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0008$; the $\kappa{=}2$ rung is below the accuracy gate). Exact invariance of the statistic neither implies suppression nor holds it fixed; the magnitude channel moves it regardless. We report this as a refutation of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s practical content, not a confirmation of its algebra.

\textbf{Mode A: the two regimes, and where they hold.} The payload dose gives Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} a single falsifiable shape: ASR single-peaked at $r{=}1$, since an over-attenuated adversary carries no payload (Regime~B) and an over-amplified one is discarded by a rank aggregator (Regime~A). It is \textbf{confirmed for \texttt{coord\_median}}, the rank aggregator the regimes are stated for: ASR rises $0.124 \to 0.315 \to 0.443$ into $r{=}1$ and falls $0.443 \to 0.296 \to 0.098$ past it, both sides significant ($p_{\downarrow}{=}3.0{\times}10^{-4}$ amplified, $6.4{\times}10^{-4}$ attenuated) at accuracy $\ge 0.74$ throughout (Figure~\ref{fig:modeA}). It is \textbf{refuted for reputation}, which is monotone: ASR is maximal at the most attenuated rung ($0.894$ at $r{=}0.135$) and near zero once $r \ge 1$, because an attenuated adversary reads as \emph{consistent} to a reputation score and stops being down-weighted. Reputation is not a rank aggregator, so the two-regime shape was never licensed for it; the split is the theorem behaving exactly where it applies and not where it does not.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.62\linewidth]{figures/targeted_modeA.pdf}
\caption{\textbf{Mode~A, the payload dose: the two mechanism-preserving regimes, and where they hold.} With dispersion fixed and only the adversary/benign weight ratio $r$ swept, \texttt{coord\_median} (a rank aggregator, the case Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s regimes are stated over) is \textbf{single-peaked at $r{=}1$} as the theorem predicts (over-attenuated adversaries carry no payload, over-amplified ones are discarded) while reputation is monotone, off-spec and refuted for that shape. Mode~A is the $\Delta_c$-\emph{swept} companion to Mode~S's $\Delta_c$-pinned design (Proposition~\ref{prop:rho_deltac}); Mode~S itself is Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}. Bars are Student-$t$ $95\%$ intervals; numbers recomputed per seed by \texttt{analyze\_targeted\_dose.py}.}
\label{fig:modeA}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[h]
\caption{Mode~S (statistic-only), $n{=}5$ per arm ($n{=}8$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}), per-attack mean ASR with the two measured channels.
