\paragraph{A pre-registered replication on a second downstream defense, and what it refutes.} One arm cannot establish which channel matters, and the two Mode-S arms above move together on both candidate predictors: reputation is higher on \emph{both} decision and admission change and also had the larger rise, so nothing in that pair separates them. \texttt{coord\_median} under the pixel backdoor is \emph{rank-discordant} and therefore does: of the interpretable Mode-S arms it has the \textbf{smallest decision change} ($0.482$) and the \textbf{largest admission change} ($0.033$), so the decision reading predicts its rise is the \emph{smallest} of the three and the admission reading predicts the \emph{largest}. It is also a structurally different defense, a coordinate-wise order statistic (Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(b)) against Krum's selector (class (c)), and it suppresses the pixel backdoor at usable accuracy, so there is suppression to lose. Thresholds were frozen against the two published rises (confirm if $\Delta > +0.178$, refute if $\Delta < +0.150$, indeterminate between) at commit \texttt{06d9bce} before \texttt{results/dose\_replication/} existed, with the identity rung imported bit-identical and $15$ new runs spent (\texttt{experiments/pre\_registration\_dose\_replication.md}).

\textbf{The outcome is $\Delta = +0.098$ at $n{=}5$: the admission \emph{ordering} is refuted.} The arm with the largest admission change rose \emph{less} than an arm with a smaller one, so \textbf{admission magnitude does not order outcomes across arms}. Neither does the decision reading, which predicted the \emph{smallest} rise of the three when $+0.098$ is the middle one, so this arm refutes a rank prediction from \emph{each} candidate channel. The pooled model agrees: re-fit with these cells it is still not confirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$; the admission coefficient's one-sided $p$ moves $0.055 \to 0.041$, but the adversarial-share coefficient is negative, so the pre-specified conjunction fails). \textbf{What does replicate is the negative}: a decision change of $0.482$ costs no suppression by the frozen margin, at mean accuracy $0.698$, so the accuracy gate does not bind and the flagship result now holds on two downstream defenses in \emph{different} invariance classes. Two qualifications, the first pre-specified in the freeze and the second post-hoc: this cell's identity ASR is $0.443$, so equivalence is to the \emph{identity rung} and not to a low absolute ASR (an ``every rung ${<}0.5$'' criterion has no headroom here and was deliberately not used); and the rise is positive in $5/5$ seeds (paired $t{=}3.39$, one-sided $p{=}0.014$, \emph{not} pre-registered), so on this class the cost of disturbing the decision is \textbf{small and consistent rather than zero}. All numbers are recomputed per seed by \texttt{analyze\_dose\_replication.py}, which imports its rung builder and trend test from the frozen suite's scorer.

\paragraph{The design choice, not the defense, sets the inferred direction.} This is the paper's central empirical result. On this identical cell (matched aggregate displacement, decision change and clean accuracy, Table~\ref{tab:comparability}), \textbf{the confounded design does not lose power, it points the other way}: at $n{=}20$ the outcome-gated ladder moves $-0.273$ ($0.478 \to 0.205$) where the instrument moves $+0.125$ ($0.478 \to 0.603$), the two paired $95\%$ intervals $[-0.334,-0.213]$ and $[+0.095,+0.155]$ disjoint and both excluding zero. Closing the attenuation channel does not sharpen a weak positive effect, it \textbf{inverts it}. \textbf{The two designs are not the same intervention}, and Table~\ref{tab:comparability} states the one non-match: $\Lambda_a$ moves in both ($0.081$ against $0.033$, both $n{=}5$), so neither column is a clean statistic-only intervention and no $p$-value attaches to the difference between them. What the contrast establishes is that the design choice sets the inferred direction, so a criterion calibrated against the confounded contrast would have been calibrated against the confound, and a cross-defense contrast would have read the fall as evidence about the statistic. That is why the identification boundary of §\ref{app:boundary} is not a technicality about power.

\paragraph{What the two arms jointly establish, stated formally.} The arms above and the class-(a) control (\texttt{cos\_krum}) are between them a \emph{constructive} answer to the question C2 was written to settle. Both halves are witnessed by runs already reported, so the following formalizes measurements rather than adding a claim.

\begin{proposition}[Statistic-level non-identifiability]
\label{prop:nonidentifiability}
Within the positive per-client rescaling class there exist transform families $T_1,T_2$ and downstream defenses $d_2$ such that
(a) $T_1$ and $T_2$ induce arbitrarily different statistics and \emph{decisions} at $d_2$ while inducing the \emph{same} admitted adversarial mass and the same suppression; and
(b) $T_1$ and $T_2$ induce the \emph{same} admitted adversarial mass (indeed the same statistic and the same decision) while inducing \emph{different} suppression.
Consequently no predicate of the downstream statistic alone determines the admitted adversarial mass, and no predicate of the admitted mass alone determines suppression.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Both halves are exhibited by runs reported in this section. (a) Mode~S into Krum under model-scaling, $\kappa{=}0$ against $\kappa{=}2$: $\rho$ moves from $1$ to $54.6$ and Krum's decision changes in up to $0.80$ of rounds, while the support of the admitted adversarial mass changes in none of the $60$ measured rounds (a floor on this arm: no adversary is admitted at any rung), the adversarial coefficient share is constant at $0.266667$, and suppression shows \textbf{no evidence of a practically meaningful change under the pre-registered $\pm0.15$ practical-equivalence margin} ($|\Delta|{=}0.026$). (b) \texttt{cos\_krum} under the pixel backdoor: by Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) its statistic is invariant, and its decision and admission are measured bit-identical ($0.000$) at every rung, yet ASR falls $0.173$ by the first rung alone, at $\rho{=}2.72$, where the accuracy gate still passes ($0.599 \to 0.567$; paired $t{=}{-}1.976$, one-sided $p{=}0.044$, falling in $5/8$ seeds).
\end{proof}

% Round 61, page gate: this sentence was the closing line of §5's design-comparison paragraph, and it
% is the ONLY statement in the corpus that a preserved statistic is not thereby uninformative. It is
% moved here rather than dropped, beside the proposition whose reading it guards, and §5 keeps the
% pointer it already carried. One home, and the home is the claim's own neighbourhood.
\noindent \textbf{What the proposition does not say.} A preserved statistic is not thereby uninformative; it is not \emph{sufficient}, remaining evidence about what $d_2$ reads and none about what it suppresses.

\noindent \textbf{The corollary is why the title says \emph{beyond} statistic preservation}: no metric defined \emph{solely} on preservation of the downstream statistic can identify attack-suppression equivalence, because (a) exhibits statistic disturbance without suppression change and (b) exhibits suppression change without statistic disturbance. \textbf{Scope, stated immediately}: this is a non-existence claim \emph{within the positive-rescaling class on these defenses and attacks}, established by exhibited counterexample: \textbf{not} a claim that no metric of any kind can order security, and not a claim about transforms outside the class.

\begin{proposition}[$\rho$ and $\Delta_c$ are independently controllable]
\label{prop:rho_deltac}
Within the same class, the statistic-level dispersion $\rho = \max_i c_i/\min_i c_i$ and the adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c = \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ are not functionally related: there is a family with $\Delta_c \equiv 0$ exactly while $\rho$ ranges over $[1, 54.6]$, and a family with the statistic's dispersion held fixed while $\Delta_c$ ranges over $[-1.138, +2.469]$. Hence neither quantity determines the other.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By construction, with the realized values tabulated in the supplement (§\ref{app:delta_c}) and computed with no ASR involved. Mode~S pins every adversarial coefficient at $c{=}1$ and spreads only the benign clients, giving $\Delta_c \equiv 0$ at every $\rho$ up to $54.6$; Mode~A holds the benign weights uniform and sweeps only the adversary-to-benign ratio $r$, moving $\Delta_c$ over $[-1.138,+2.469]$ at fixed statistic dispersion.
\end{proof}

\noindent This is why the two instruments were the right ones, and it makes the supplement's §\ref{sec:rho_vs_deltac} warning precise: \textbf{a transform reported by $\rho$ alone leaves $\Delta_c$ entirely free}, so $\rho$ cannot be a sufficient statistic for what the transform does to a defense. It does \emph{not} follow that $\Delta_c$ is a predictor: §\ref{sec:dose_outcome} reports that its magnitude fails to order suppression either.

\paragraph{The dissociation is not Krum-specific: all four aggregators, all four channels.} Table~\ref{tab:channels_full} collects the endpoint change ($\kappa{=}0$ against $\kappa{=}2$) in each measured channel under Mode~S for every downstream defense in the paper. \textbf{Recomputed, not transcribed}: every cell is emitted by \texttt{build\_channel\_table.py}, which only reads frozen artifacts under \texttt{results/}, \texttt{admission\_\allowbreak measurement.json}, \texttt{femnist\_\allowbreak admission.json} and \texttt{resnet18\_\allowbreak admission.json} for the channel columns, the \texttt{targeted\_dose/}, \texttt{dose\_\allowbreak replication/}, \texttt{dose\_femnist/} and \texttt{dose\_\allowbreak resnet18/} summaries for the ASR column; it also asserts that the identity rung displaces nothing ($0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ for all seven rows). The sixth row's aggregate column comes from \texttt{displacement\_\allowbreak decomposition.json} and its ASR from \texttt{score\_only/summary.json}, both read-only; the emitter refuses to merge that arm into the ASR sources it shares cell keys with, so it cannot shadow the first row's published number. The influence column is \textbf{aggregator-specific by necessity} (a selector has no weights) and its instantiation is: adversarial share of the Weiszfeld/reputation weight mass for the averager, adversarial share of selected coordinates for the order statistic, and the indicator that an adversarial update is selected at all for the two selectors. The fifth row replicates the first row's arm on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN (EMNIST-byclass with \texttt{simple\_cnn}) with \textbf{its channels measured prospectively, before its ASR existed}; the result is reported below. \textbf{The seventh row replicates it with architecture varying alone} (CIFAR-10 with \texttt{resnet18}), its channels likewise measured before its ASR existed, and it is the one row that shares \emph{both} the first row's dataset and its cell key: the ASR lookup is keyed on the triple (dataset, model, cell) precisely so that the two cannot shadow each other, and the emitter raises rather than resolving a collision by file order (§\ref{app:resnet18_dose}). \textbf{What the $\Delta$~adm.\ column pools}: the four \texttt{cifar\_cnn} CIFAR-10 rows contribute $60$ measured rounds each, $240$ in total, and adversarial mass is present at baseline in $100$ of those $240$ rounds (including the $48$ per arm where reputation and \texttt{coord\_median} carry it) while its support moves in none of them, which is the count Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}'s annotation reports and why that annotation carries no single arm's name.

\paragraph{A second dataset and a differently configured CNN, pre-registered.} The fifth row's arm is the first row's arm re-run with EMNIST-byclass (62 classes, $1{\times}28{\times}28$) and \texttt{simple\_cnn} in place of CIFAR-10 and \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, everything else held: Mode~S, Krum as $d_2$, the committed model-scaling attack, the same four frozen rungs $\kappa \in \{0,0.5,1,2\}$. The decision rule lives in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_dose\_femnist.md}, git-committed before any EMNIST-byclass ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash.

\textbf{One naming correction, made here rather than left to be inferred.} Earlier drafts labelled this arm ``FEMNIST'' in the tables while the prose said EMNIST-byclass, and the tables were the wrong ones: the \texttt{femnist} key loads \emph{torchvision} \texttt{EMNIST(split="byclass")} and partitions it with this paper's own label-Dirichlet split, \textbf{not} LEAF FEMNIST's partition by writer. So the arm is a second dataset and a differently configured CNN, and it is \textbf{not} a more federated partition than the CIFAR-10 arm; it is the same partition scheme on different data. Every table now reads EMNIST-byclass. The frozen artifact paths, the pre-registration filename and the JSON dataset key retain the \texttt{femnist} spelling because renaming a committed artifact would break the provenance the rest of this appendix rests on.

\textbf{The premise was measured first, with no ASR anywhere.} Krum's selection changes on $0.643$--$0.714$ of rounds across the three disturbed rungs while the support of the adversarial mass it admits is unchanged in all $56$ measured rounds (again a floor: no adversary is admitted at any rung), so the arm has a real disturbance to test. Had the decision change come out near zero the arm would have been reported ineligible rather than run: that is the \texttt{cos\_krum} failure mode, and this check is what distinguishes the two.

\textbf{Suppression shows no evidence of a practically meaningful change.} $\Delta = -0.017$ (mean ASR $0.027 \to 0.010$) against the frozen $\pm 0.15$ practical-equivalence margin, beside CIFAR-10's $-0.026$ on the same cell, with mean clean accuracy \emph{rising} $0.767 \to 0.835$ so the accuracy floor binds on no rung. Under the frozen rule this is a \textbf{replication on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN}. \textbf{That is narrower than the wording the pre-registration froze}, which reads ``a second dataset and architecture'': \texttt{simple\_cnn} and \texttt{cifar\_cnn} are two configurations of one shallow-convnet family, so we report the narrower phrase and leave the frozen file unedited, claiming less than a rule licenses being always permitted where claiming more is not. The prohibition we keep verbatim is the one that matters: never ``generalizes'', which one further dataset does not license.

\textbf{Three disclosures, each of them from the freeze rather than from hindsight.} (i) $n{=}3$ seeds per rung, not $5$, fixed in advance to match the EMNIST-byclass payoff matrix's own trial budget. \textbf{The frozen verdict is that $n{=}3$ one, and the same ladder also reads at $n{=}5$ with no new run needed}: the comparability amendment of §\ref{app:sixcell} ran seeds $45$ and $46$ of this exact cell at all four rungs, so pooling them with the frozen three, frozen rows winning and the frozen directory never written, gives $\Delta{=}{-}0.013$ beside $-0.017$, mean ASR $0.024 \to 0.011$ at accuracy $0.758 \to 0.841$: the same label under the same unmoved margin. Two more seeds \emph{widen} the interval across zero, $[-0.034,+0.009]$ against $n{=}3$'s $[-0.033,-0.001]$, so they remove an apparent small fall rather than confirm one, and \textbf{one of the five carries the opposite sign} (seed $45$, $+0.015$, its identity rung landing at ASR $0.000018$), which a 3-seed mean cannot show. Both counts are reported and neither replaces the other (\texttt{analyze\_dose\_femnist.py --pooled}). Those two seeds were pre-registered in the comparability document before they ran and are post-hoc in this arm's own. (ii) The secondary Jonckheere--Terpstra test was specified to detect a monotone \emph{rise} (the outcome that would have refuted the flat prediction with attenuation already closed by construction) and the observed trend is a \emph{fall} ($J{=}16.0$, $z{=}{-}1.563$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.941$; at $n{=}5$, $J{=}57.0$, $z{=}{-}1.211$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.887$, the same direction). We report it and we do \textbf{not} score it as support for flatness; the pre-registration states in advance that at $n{=}3$ a null from this test is not evidence of flatness, and two more seeds do not convert one into evidence. (iii) The two branches had unequal headroom. With the identity rung at ASR $0.027$, refuting us required mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) > 0.177$ against a ceiling of $1$ and was fully reachable, whereas the indeterminate branch ($\Delta < -0.15$) was arithmetically unreachable. \textbf{Equivalence here is to the identity rung, not to a low absolute ASR.}

\textbf{Harness sanity, which is an identity check and not corroboration.} The $\kappa{=}0$ rung is identity$\circ$Krum, i.e.\ Krum alone, and it lands \emph{bit-identically} on the EMNIST-byclass Krum figure from our earlier payoff-matrix sweep ($0.0269784552$ at accuracy $0.7670795973$). Both pipelines are deterministic given a seed and both run three trials from base seed $42$, so the agreement shows they execute the same protocol rather than a similar one: the strongest form the pre-registered check can take, and for the same reason \textbf{not} independent corroboration of that figure: the same computation run twice cannot corroborate itself. \textbf{The pooled seeds of disclosure~\emph{(i)} now carry the same check across two runners.} Having already read those two seeds from the comparability suite, we ran this arm's own runner over the same eight $(\text{rung},\text{seed})$ cells, pre-registered as an amendment at \texttt{ad5479b} before those runs: it reproduces all eight to ten decimal places, largest gap $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$, so the pooled reading is not an artifact of which suite measured it (\texttt{analyze\_dose\_femnist.py --pooled}). The same reading applies: identical protocols, not independent confirmation.

\paragraph{A second architecture family, pre-registered, with the gate scored first.} The seventh row's arm is the first row's arm with \textbf{architecture varying alone}: \texttt{resnet18} (11.2M parameters, BatchNorm replaced by GroupNorm-8, \texttt{conv1} adapted to $32{\times}32$, \texttt{maxpool} $\to$ Identity) in place of \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, and CIFAR-10, $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, $\alpha$, the committed model-scaling attack and Krum as $d_2$ all at their frozen values. The decision rule is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_dose\_resnet18.md}, git-committed at \texttt{89046f6} before any ResNet18 ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash. \textbf{Two endpoint rungs only}, $\kappa \in \{0,2\}$ of the frozen four-rung ladder ($\rho = 1.00 \to 54.60$), at seeds $42$--$44$: the interior rungs are not run, \textbf{so this arm carries no trend statistic at all} and the runner prints that restriction rather than leaving it to be inferred.

\textbf{The gate was scored before the ladder, and it is what makes the arm interpretable.} No ResNet18/Krum figure existed on disk, so the pre-registration named the void branch first: unless Krum alone suppresses the committed scaling attack on this architecture (mean ASR $< 0.5$ at mean clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$) \emph{there is no suppression for the ladder to preserve and the arm is void, not negative}. It passed: at $\kappa{=}0$, mean ASR $0.071$ at mean clean accuracy $0.536$, $n{=}3$. Had it failed, the runner refuses the $\kappa{=}2$ leg outright rather than spending it, and we would be reporting a void.

\textbf{Suppression does not rise, and under the rule this arm was frozen against the negative replicates on a second architecture family.} $\Delta = -0.041$: mean ASR $0.071$ at $\kappa{=}0$ against $0.030$ at $\kappa{=}2$, its magnitude inside the frozen $\pm0.15$ practical-equivalence margin, beside \texttt{cifar\_cnn}'s $-0.026$ and EMNIST-byclass's $-0.017$ on the same cell. \textbf{We do not write ``shows no evidence of a practically meaningful change'' about this arm, and disclosure~\emph{(v)} is why}: §\ref{app:sensitivity} defines that phrase by the paired $95\%$ interval lying inside the margin, and at $n{=}3$ this arm's does not. \textbf{Both endpoints are $n{=}3$ on the same three seeds}, so the difference of rung means and the paired mean difference are the same number to four places ($-0.0410$), and \texttt{seeds\_match} is true on the row; no leg is a mixed-$n$ comparison. Mean clean accuracy \emph{rises} $0.536 \to 0.557$, so the $0.35$ floor binds on neither rung. Under the frozen three-way rule ($|\Delta| < 0.15$ replicates, $\Delta > +0.15$ architecture-specific, $\Delta < -0.15$ a fall) this is a \textbf{replication across architecture families}, and it is the arm that earns the phrase: \texttt{simple\_cnn} and \texttt{cifar\_cnn} are two configurations of one shallow-convnet family, whereas an 11.2M-parameter residual network is not.

\textbf{Five disclosures about this arm, four of them limits.} (i) \textbf{The channels were measured prospectively and the admission column is vacuous here}, exactly as on rows~1 and~5. \texttt{results/\allowbreak resnet18\_\allowbreak admission.json} records ``No ASR. Freezes nothing.'' and predates the ladder; it reads the decision disturbed and the admitted adversarial mass not ($\Delta$~dec.\ $0.556$ against $\Delta$~adm.\ $0.000$ at the top rung), which is the premise the flagship arm rests on and the reason the cell was eligible at all. \textbf{But adversarial mass is never present at baseline in any of this arm's $72$ measured rounds}, so its $\Delta$~adm.\ $=0.000$ is vacuous and we do not report it as evidence; \texttt{count\_admission\_rounds.py} prints that verdict per row rather than letting a zero speak for itself. (ii) \textbf{Clean accuracy is far below this architecture's own reach}: $0.536$--$0.557$ against the $0.66$--$0.76$ the ResNet18 mini-sweep of §\ref{app:resnet18_replication_details} attains in the same regime, ResNet18 at $50$ rounds being far from converged. The gate is a floor and not a claim of a converged model, and no statement here is about converged training. (iii) \textbf{One cell, one attack, and it widens nothing else.} It does not add a dataset, an $N$, a $K$, an $f$, a round count or an attack, it carries no composition suite, and it does not touch the sign-reversal cell of §\ref{app:sixcell}. (iv) \textbf{Why this arm fits an overnight budget when §\ref{app:sixcell}'s disclosure~\emph{(iv)} prices ResNet18 at $65$~h}: that figure is a \emph{comparability} cell, two designs $\times$ four rungs $\times$ five seeds $=40$ runs at ${\approx}5900$~s. This arm is one design $\times$ two endpoint rungs $\times$ three seeds $=6$ runs, which is $13.4$~h as measured. The two numbers price different objects and neither supersedes the other. (v) \textbf{The interval is wider than the margin, so the frozen $\pm0.15$ is load-bearing for this arm and for no other.} The paired $95\%$ interval is $[-0.183,+0.101]$ (per-seed ASR $0.114/0.050/0.048$ at $\kappa{=}0$ against $0.008/0.034/0.047$ at $\kappa{=}2$ on seeds $42$--$44$, hence per-seed differences $-0.106$, $-0.017$, $-0.000$ at $\mathrm{sd}\,0.057$; the Reproducibility statement's per-seed promise covers this arm too, and these are those values), so this arm's $m^{\ast} = \max(|\mathrm{lo}|,|\mathrm{hi}|)$ is $0.183$ against the comparability ladder's binding $0.034$ (§\ref{app:sensitivity}), and the body's ladder claim is scoped to Table~\ref{tab:margin_ladder} for that reason rather than stated of the paper as a whole. TOST against the frozen margin \emph{does} reject, at the $90\%$ interval $\alpha{=}0.05$ reads: $p{=}0.040$, post hoc, the closest call in Table~\ref{tab:tost}, and the smallest margin at which it still rejects is $0.137$. \textbf{So this arm replicates under the rule it was frozen against and establishes equivalence at no tighter margin}; three seeds on two rungs is what that buys, and the remedy is seeds rather than wording.
\label{app:resnet18_dose}

\textbf{What the instrument does \emph{not} hold fixed, named in full, and what it pins exactly.} Mode~S pins the \emph{adversarial} coefficients; it does not pin the benign ones and was never claimed to. So the dose also moves (a) Krum's pairwise distances, hence the statistic itself; (b) \emph{which} benign client Krum selects, in $0.533/0.800/0.733$ of rounds at $\rho{=}2.72/7.39/54.6$; (c) the \textbf{magnitude and geometry of the update actually aggregated}, since the selected client enters scaled by its own coefficient; and (d) the model trajectory, which diverges across rungs in consequence. Channel (c) is not a conjecture but a \textbf{number we already measure and print}: the $\Delta$~agg.\ column of Table~\ref{tab:channels_full} is $\|\mathrm{agg}(T(U)) - \mathrm{agg}(U)\|/\|\mathrm{agg}(U)\|$, the relative displacement of the aggregate the defense \emph{emits}, and it reads $\mathbf{0.892}$ on this cell. The supplement §\ref{app:decomposition} splits that $0.892$, as vectors, into $0.986$ of \emph{re-selection} (a different benign client is chosen, and in $0/11$ of the rounds where that happens it is an adversary) and $0.973$ of \emph{rescaling} (the chosen client arrives scaled by its own coefficient); for \texttt{cos\_krum} the displacement is \emph{entirely} rescaling, since $0/15$ selections change at any rung. The claim this arm licenses is therefore sharper than a claim about the statistic alone: \textbf{a $0.892$ displacement of the emitted aggregate, together with decision changes in up to $80\%$ of rounds, leaves suppression inside the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin, provided the adversarial coefficient share does not move.} \textbf{What is pinned is pinned exactly}: $c_{\mathrm{adv}}{=}1.0$ at every rung with maximum deviation $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ and the share constant to $3.5\mathrm{e}{-}07$ on CIFAR-10, $3.9\mathrm{e}{-}07$ on EMNIST-byclass: float32 norm read-back noise, not slack in the instrument (\texttt{measure\_admission.py}). Naming (a)--(d) is what keeps the claim to the one channel the instrument closes by construction.

\paragraph{Closing channel (c) as well: the pre-registered score-only control.} Channel (c) (the magnitude and geometry of what is actually aggregated) is the one confound Mode~S leaves open, so we closed it too rather than argue about it. \textbf{Score-only Krum} scores on the transformed stack exactly as before and then aggregates the \emph{untransformed} selected update: the dose can change \emph{which} client contributes and nothing whatever about \emph{what} that client contributes. Statistic and decision are disturbed identically to the uncontrolled arm (the selection is computed on the same transformed stack, so $\Delta$~dec.\ is the same $0.733$) while $\Delta$~agg.\ is no longer $0.892$ but the re-selection component alone, $0.986$ relative to $\|u_{s_0}\|$ (the supplement §\ref{app:decomposition}), the rescaling component being closed by construction. This is the reviewer-suggested control in its own terms: hold the selected update's magnitude and direction fixed while perturbing the selection statistic independently.

\textbf{Frozen before the arm existed.} The decision rule is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_score\_only.md}, git-committed at \texttt{35788d9} before any score-only ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its own \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash. \emph{Primary:} $\Delta = {}$mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) - {}$mean ASR$(\kappa{=}0)$ against the suite's existing $\pm 0.15$ practical-equivalence margin, on \texttt{krum} / \texttt{committed\_scaling} / CIFAR-10 / \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, the same four rungs and seeds 42--46. $\Delta > +0.15$ would have said that \textbf{the published flat result was partly an artifact of the magnitude channel} and that the paper's central claim narrows to ``with benign magnitudes free to move'': that branch is why the arm was worth three hours of compute. The $\kappa{=}0$ rung is imported rather than re-run, because at the identity the transform returns the stack unwrapped and score-only Krum \emph{is} Krum alone; that is an exact claim, not an approximation, and it was verified bit-identically at one seed before the arm started.

\textbf{The negative survives with the magnitude channel closed.} $\Delta = \mathbf{-0.023}$ (mean ASR $0.062 \to 0.039$) against $-0.026$ for the uncontrolled arm on the identical cell: \textbf{closing the magnitude channel moves the effect by $0.003$}. The refuting threshold $0.212$ is nowhere approached, and mean clean accuracy clears the $0.35$ floor at every rung ($0.565/0.629/0.624/0.630$), so no rung is a collapsed model. \textbf{The central claim is therefore instrumented against two independent confounds} (attenuation, closed by Mode~S, and magnitude, closed here) and the sharpened statement is that a $0.986$ displacement of the emitted aggregate together with decision changes in $0.733$ of rounds leaves suppression inside the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin.

\textbf{Three disclosures, from the freeze rather than from hindsight.} (i) The secondary Jonckheere--Terpstra test was specified to detect a monotone \emph{rise} (the outcome that would have refuted flatness with attenuation already closed) and the observed trend is a \emph{fall} ($J{=}58.5$, $z{=}{-}1.110$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.867$, permutation $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.874$). We report it and do \textbf{not} score it as support for flatness; the pre-registration states in advance that at $n{=}5$ a null from this test is not evidence of flatness. (ii) The two branches had unequal headroom. With the identity rung at $0.062$, refuting us required mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) > 0.212$ against a ceiling of $1$ and was fully reachable, whereas the indeterminate branch ($\Delta < -0.15$) was \emph{arithmetically unreachable}: ASR cannot fall below $0$, so the largest possible fall on this cell is $0.062$. (iii) \textbf{Selectors only.} A weighted averager and a coordinate-wise order statistic emit no single selected client, so ``the selected update'' is undefined for them and the control does not cover them, which means the \texttt{coord\_median} arm carrying the $+0.098$ rise ($n{=}5$) is \textbf{not} magnitude-controlled, and neither is reputation's $+0.178$ ($n{=}5$). Score-only Krum is also an instrument and not a defense: it aggregates an update its own scoring stage never saw.

\textbf{The corollary the pre-registration staked on \texttt{cos\_krum}, and exactly what it settles.} Section~5 of the same document asserted, before the run, that score-only \texttt{cos\_krum} must be \emph{bit-identical} across rungs: its statistic is exactly invariant under positive rescaling, so the selection cannot move, and with the emitted update untransformed the whole 50-round trajectory must repeat. It does (accuracy $0.528400$ and ASR $0.003111$ at all four rungs, $|\Delta| = 0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ throughout) and the $\kappa{=}0$ value reproduces the published cell at that seed exactly, so the two pipelines agree at $0.000000$ rather than approximately. \textbf{What the check does not do is confirm the arm's published fall.} It runs one seed, as frozen, and that seed is unrepresentative of the arm: under the uncontrolled dose its own ladder \emph{rises} $+0.224$ where the eight-seed mean \emph{falls} $-0.425$. Closing the magnitude channel removes all $0.224$ of that seed's movement, which corroborates the \emph{channel} (for \texttt{cos\_krum} the emitted displacement is entirely rescaling, so no other route is open) but attributing the arm's mean fall to magnitude rests on that decomposition rather than on this check, and settling it empirically would need the arm's other seven seeds. Expanding a pre-registered seed count after seeing the data is precisely what pre-registration exists to prevent, so we report the invariance as confirmed and the arm-level attribution as \textbf{corroborated mechanically, not established at the arm mean}.

\subsubsection{The 2$\times$2 channel factorial, and the decomposition it refuses to license}
\label{app:factorial}
Mode~S opens two channels into a selector: the \emph{statistic} channel (the dose moves Krum's pairwise distances, so \emph{which} client is selected changes) and the \emph{magnitude} channel (the selected client enters scaled by its own coefficient). Score-only closes the second. The fourth cell closes the first instead: \textbf{emit-only Krum} scores the \emph{untransformed} stack, pinning the decision to the identity rung's, and aggregates the \emph{transformed} selected update. The four cells are then a factorial, and if the two channels acted independently the three measured effects would satisfy $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}} = \Delta_{\mathrm{SO}} + \Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}$. \textbf{They do not.} Rules were frozen in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_emit\_only.md} at \texttt{b995f1b} before any emit-only ASR existed, with the runner refusing to start unless its own \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash; the $\kappa{=}0$ rung is imported rather than re-run, because at the identity the transform returns the stack unwrapped and emit-only \emph{is} Krum alone, an exact claim verified bit-identically before the arm started. All three harness checks the freeze required pass at $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ across five published arms and the score-only control, so the refactor that added this cell perturbed no published computation.

\begin{center}\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{5pt}
\begin{tabular}{llcrr}
\toprule
Cell & statistic channel & magnitude channel & mean ASR & $\Delta$ASR \\
\midrule
$\kappa{=}0$ identity (imported) & closed & closed & $0.062$ & --- \\
Score-only & \textbf{open} & closed & $0.032$ & $-0.030$ \\
Emit-only & closed & \textbf{open} & $0.107$ & $+0.046$ \\
Full Mode~S & \textbf{open} & \textbf{open} & $0.023$ & $-0.039$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{4}{l}{additive prediction $\Delta_{\mathrm{SO}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}$} & $+0.016$ \\
\multicolumn{4}{l}{residual against measured $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}}$ (frozen margin $0.05$)} & $\mathbf{-0.054}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent \textbf{The table above is the published $n{=}5$ design, retained as the pre-registration requires; the verdict it produced has since been withdrawn by our own top-up.} Seeds $47$--$61$ were added under rules frozen at \texttt{684b31e}, and at $n{=}20$ $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.010$ ($95\%$ CI $[-0.028,+0.047]$), so the magnitude channel is \emph{established} inert rather than merely point-inside, while the residual is $-0.019$ ($[-0.061,+0.023]$), \textbf{inside} the additivity margin. \textbf{So the $n{=}5$ reading that the channels interact does not survive}, and what remains is \emph{consistent with} separability and not an establishment of it, the interval reaching $-0.061$ and so not contained in $\pm0.05$. Its half-width, $0.042$, in fact came in \emph{narrower} than the $0.057$ that top-up's own pre-registration projected, so what blocks containment here is the off-centre point estimate rather than the width the projection worried about. Read the $n{=}5$ paragraphs below as the record of what was published and why it was fragile, not as the current verdict.

\noindent \textbf{The primary rung is forced, and the additive model gets the sign wrong.} At $\kappa{=}2$ mean clean accuracy is $0.333$, below the $0.35$ floor, so the primary contrast is reported at $\kappa{=}1$ and \textbf{that substitution is forced by the floor rather than chosen after seeing ASR}: Section~5 of the pre-registration named $\kappa{=}2$ as the rung at risk, and named it for this cell specifically, because emit-only aggregates the \emph{rescaled} update and so degrades training in a way the other three cells do not. Seed-matched at $\kappa{=}1$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.046$ sits inside the frozen inert margin of $0.05$, so \textbf{the magnitude channel is inert on its own}, which is what we predicted. But $\Delta_{\mathrm{SO}} + \Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.016$ against a measured $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}} = -0.039$: an additivity residual of $-0.054$, outside the frozen $0.05$ margin, and \textbf{the additive model does not merely mis-size the joint effect, it reverses its sign}. So \textbf{no per-channel decomposition of the uncontrolled arm is licensed}, and we withdraw any reading of the re-selection/rescaling split as a decomposition of suppression. That split remains sound as the thing it says it is: the supplement's algebraic decomposition (§\ref{app:decomposition}) decomposes a \emph{displacement}, and says in its own terms that it makes no claim about suppression.

\noindent \textbf{Three reasons this verdict is fragile, all of them ours to report.} (i) It is knife-edge on both margins at once, $|\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}|$ falling \emph{inside} the inert margin by $0.004$ and the residual \emph{outside} the additivity margin by $0.004$. (ii) Both pre-registered secondaries are null: the emit-only per-seed sign count is $3$ up against $2$ down, and Jonckheere--Terpstra across the four rungs gives $z = -0.437$, $p_{\uparrow} = 0.669$. (iii) One seed carries the result. Seed~42's emit-only run collapses to clean accuracy $0.2613$, below our floor \emph{at that seed} though the rung mean of $0.487$ clears it, and its $\Delta$ is $+0.272$ against $-0.007$, $+0.007$, $+0.013$, $-0.058$; excluding it would give $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = -0.011$ and residual $-0.003$, reading as separable. \textbf{We do not exclude it}: Section~5 fixes $n{=}5$ on seeds $42$--$46$ and applies the floor to a rung's \emph{mean} rather than per seed, so no per-seed exclusion is licensed, and the pre-registered top-up settled it without one. \textbf{At $n{=}20$ the knife edge is gone and the verdict it produced is withdrawn}: $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.010$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.028,+0.047]$, so the magnitude channel is \emph{established} inert, and the residual is $-0.019$, $[-0.061,+0.023]$, inside the additivity margin, so the interaction reading does not survive. Seed~42 remains the only draw of $20$ past either outlier threshold and is still excluded from nothing; realized sd falls $0.130 \to 0.080$. Separability is now \emph{consistent with} and \textbf{still not established}, its interval reaching $-0.061$ and so not contained in $\pm0.05$ even though its half-width, $0.042$, came in \emph{narrower} than the $0.057$ that top-up's pre-registration projected at this variance, and dropping the one seed that produces the unwelcome answer is precisely what pre-registration exists to prevent. Section~6.1 of the same document declared, before the run, that at $n{=}5$ only a large $|\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}|$ would be decisive, and $|\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}|$ is not large. \textbf{We therefore report this arm as not decisive, and act on it only in the direction that withdraws a claim}: low power is a reason to stop asserting a decomposition, never a reason to assert one. Every number above is emitted by \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak analyze\_emit\_only\_factorial.py}, which reads only frozen artifacts and asserts all four primary quantities reproduce from the per-seed cells to $<10^{-9}$ before printing. \textbf{Selectors only}: the factorial is defined for \texttt{krum} and \texttt{cos\_krum}, the harness raising for any other $d_2$, so it is one selector family, one attack, one dataset, one architecture.

\textbf{What this settles, and what it does not.} The intervention delivers the controlled isolation of C2 the two cross-defense suites could not: within a single defense, with attenuation excluded by construction \emph{and}, in the score-only arm, the magnitude of the aggregated update held exactly fixed as well, \textbf{substantial disturbance of the statistic $d_2$ reads need not produce a corresponding change in suppression once the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed} (on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S and, by pre-registered replication, on a downstream defense in a different invariance class, on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN, and on a second architecture family with architecture varying alone) while \emph{admission} of adversarial input is the channel the evidence points to. The statistic-level reading the framework was first written in is refuted; the admission-level reading is confirmed as a \emph{direction} in these interventions rather than established in general. It does \emph{not} rescue the framework's positive direction wholesale: the pooled two-channel model over all $26$ cells is \textbf{not} confirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$), and the replication refutes its cross-arm ordering outright, so admission is a better single predictor than the statistic without being a quantitative one. It addresses neither recall nor discovery (FG$\to$RFA remains a false negative), and nothing in §\ref{sec:dose_outcome} is rescored: its pooled refutation, its two refuted arms and its indeterminate arm stand exactly as reported.
\paragraph{Mode~M: coordinate masking, a second transformation class.} Every other arm in this paper transforms client updates by a positive per-client scalar, the family Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance} classifies and Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} bounds. \textbf{Mode~M} is outside it. Benign client $i$ has a random $m$ fraction of the coordinates of each parameter tensor zeroed, drawn from an independent Bernoulli$(1{-}m)$ seeded by \texttt{default\_rng([seed, round, slot])} so it is reproducible and uncorrelated with adversary status, and the surviving coordinates are then \textbf{renormalized to the update's original $L_2$ norm}. Renormalization is load-bearing and was required in the pre-registration \emph{before any ASR existed}: an unnormalized mask shrinks benign norms and re-opens the magnitude channel that §\ref{app:score_only}'s control exists to close. The unnormalized variant was considered and rejected for that reason, and the rejection is recorded rather than omitted. \textbf{Adversarial updates are returned unmodified, exactly}: the maximum deviation of every adversarial coefficient from $1$ is $0.00$ with object identity preserved, a strictly stronger pinning than Mode~S's $3.5{\times}10^{-7}$ float32 read-back. Rungs are $m \in \{0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.8\}$ and $m{=}0$ returns the update list unwrapped, so the identity rung is bit-identical to Krum standalone by construction; we check rather than assert this, the identity rung agreeing to $10^{-9}$ across all three directories that carry those seeds.

\textbf{The channels were measured before any ASR existed}, as the EMNIST-byclass arm's were. Krum's decision changes in $0.000/0.133/0.400/0.467$ of rounds across the ladder while the adversarial mass it admits is $0.000$ at every rung, at an aggregate displacement of $1.095$ against Mode~S's $0.892$ at a \emph{larger} decision change. So the arm was established prospectively as a generalization test, and the pre-registration (\texttt{684b31e}) then split four ways, including two that would have refuted us: a monotone rise in $m$ would confirm the statistic reading, and masking that made Krum admit the adversary would void the equivalence claim at that rung and confirm the \emph{admission} reading. \textbf{Neither fired.} At $n{=}10$ (seeds $42$--$51$) the paired contrast $m{=}0 \to m{=}0.8$ is $\Delta=-0.016$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.051,+0.019]$, contained in the frozen $\pm0.15$ margin at a half-width $23.1\%$ of it (TOST $p{=}5.3{\times}10^{-6}$), every rung mean clearing the $0.5$ ceiling and the $0.35$ accuracy floor, and Jonckheere--Terpstra finding no increasing trend ($p_{\uparrow}{=}0.895$). The mask analogue of the score-only control gives $-0.018$, $[-0.047,+0.010]$, agreeing with the full ladder to $0.002$: since adversarial updates are unmasked in \emph{both} conditions the two ladders differ only when a benign client is selected, so their agreement localizes the absent effect to \emph{which client Krum picks} rather than to the content it emits.

\textbf{What this arm does and does not license.} Only the flat-with-admission-unchanged outcome generalizes the negative, and that is the outcome observed; had admission moved, the arm would have been reported as a confirmation of the admission reading instead. \textbf{One mask is one transformation, not the class of non-rescalings}: rotations, projections, quantization and sign compression are untested, so the supportable claim is that the negative replicates on a second, structurally different transformation class, never that it holds for arbitrary upstream processing. Mode~M has nothing to impose in $24/120$ measured rung-rounds, identical to Mode~S's figure, and like Mode~S it is an \emph{instrument} and not a defense, since it reads adversary identity. The \texttt{cos\_krum} mask ladder was considered and dropped for compute before any ASR existed, which is why the masking column carries one outcome and three channel-only entries.

\subsection{Seven cells, and a pre-registered mechanism that did not survive them}
\label{app:sixcell}

\noindent \emph{Is the sign reversal a property of the design, or of this aggregator and attack?}
Table~\ref{tab:comparability} contrasts the two designs on the one arm that motivated the instrument. That contrast is one cell, and a single cell cannot say whether the sign reversal is a property of the evaluation design or of \texttt{coord\_median} under a pixel backdoor. We therefore ran the contrast on seven cells: four that already existed in frozen artifacts and are \textbf{re-scored, never re-run}, and three that did not exist and were run for this purpose ($60$ runs for the first two of them, plus $8$ under Amendment~2 and $40$ for the third under Amendment~4 below, which is the run budget that amendment froze).

\textbf{Both designs are the same computation.} The two ladders differ in exactly two tokens, the family name and whether \texttt{generic\_compose} receives an \texttt{adv\_mask}; data partition, participant draw, attack, local training and seeds are identical. \texttt{run\_comparability\_cells.py} refuses to start unless it first reproduces a published \texttt{results/dose\_response/} cell bit-identically ($|\text{diff}| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$), so the new cells are the same experiment as the four they are compared against, and \texttt{analyze\_comparability.py} refuses to print any pooled number unless all four published contrasts reproduce.

\textbf{What was frozen, and when.} Before any new run, we pre-registered a mechanism (\texttt{experiments/pre\_\allowbreak registration\_comparability.md}, frozen at \texttt{c986ef4}; two amendments at \texttt{1026a96} and \texttt{f16083b}; the three hashes are kept because every other pre-registration in this paper carries its own, and dropping them for brevity would make this the one prereg citation a reader cannot check): the two designs disagree exactly where the downstream defense admits adversarial mass, gated on the admission-level change $\Delta\Lambda_a$ at the top rung. The four existing cells separate perfectly under that rule, which is precisely why \textbf{they are training data and cannot also be evidence for it}. The rule mechanically generated a prediction for each new cell, committed in advance: DISAGREE for \texttt{coord\_median}/scaling ($\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.0086$, disclosed at the time as the weakest margin of any cell), AGREE for \texttt{krum}/EMNIST-byclass ($\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.0000$). The document names in advance what would refute it and commits to reporting refutation rather than fitting a threshold.

\begin{table}[t]
\centering
\caption{\textbf{The confounded ladder against the Mode-S instrument, on seven cells.} $\Delta$~ASR from $\kappa{=}0$ to $\kappa{=}2$, paired $95\%$ $t$ intervals, each design at its own full $n$, \textbf{which the $n$ column now prints as confounded/controlled} so the strongest row and the weakest are distinguishable on the page. \textbf{Row~4, the sign reversal, is the one row at $n{=}20$ on both legs}, following a pre-registered endpoint top-up (seeds $47$--$61$, \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_reversal\_seed\_topup.md}); at the frozen $n{=}5$ it read $-0.272$ {$[-0.422,-0.122]$} against $+0.098$ {$[+0.018,+0.178]$}, the same verdict by the same definition. \textbf{AGREE} := intervals overlap and point estimates share a sign, or both lie within $\pm0.05$ of zero; \textbf{DISAGREE} := intervals disjoint or signs opposite; \textbf{SIGN REVERSAL} := signs differ \emph{and} both intervals exclude zero. The four unmarked cells are the training data the rule was read off. \textbf{The rule predicted AGREE for the EMNIST cell and the designs disagree, so it is refuted}; per the frozen clause it is withdrawn, the four-cell pattern is demoted to a description, and the table is reported in full. \textbf{The CIFAR-100 cell carries no prediction and its $\Delta\Lambda_a$ entry is absent rather than zero}, because the rule that would have generated one was already dead when that cell was pre-registered (Amendment~4); it is scored on the two endpoint rungs the primary contrast reads, and because no trend secondary was pre-registered for this table, the trend across all four rungs is reported as a \emph{post hoc} description below rather than as a test here. \textbf{The out-of-sample result: one confirms, one refutes, one was never predicted} (Table~\ref{tab:sixcell}). \texttt{coord\_median} under \emph{model scaling} disagrees, as predicted, on the weakest margin in the set: the outcome-gated ladder reads $+0.002$ $[-0.078,+0.081]$ where the instrument reads $+0.196$ $[+0.112,+0.280]$. This is the cell that matters for the headline, because it is the paper's sign-reversal aggregator under the \emph{other} committed attack, and it says \textbf{the disagreement is not specific to the pixel backdoor}. It is a disagreement, not a second sign reversal, and we do not call it one. The EMNIST-byclass cell then \textbf{refutes the rule}: $\Delta\Lambda_a$ is exactly $0.0000$, the rule predicts the designs coincide, and they do not ($+0.157$ against $-0.013$, opposite signs). \textbf{Those opposite signs are not a second sign reversal, and we do not count them as one:} both intervals contain zero ($[-0.175,+0.489]$ and $[-0.034,+0.009]$), so neither estimate is separated from no effect and what this cell establishes is a failed prediction of the rule, not a reversal of the measured one. Whether the \emph{reversal} travels is a different question from whether the \emph{rule} does, and the third out-of-sample cell is the one that addresses it.

\textbf{The third out-of-sample cell moves the sign reversal to a third dataset, and it replicates} (Amendment~4, committed at \texttt{9b8a395} before the runner was pointed at CIFAR-100). The objection this cell answers is one we sharpen rather than defend: before this row the table already spanned two attacks, two datasets and two CNN configurations, but \textbf{the sign reversal itself did not}. It existed on CIFAR-10 with \texttt{cifar\_cnn} and nowhere else, because the cell that added a second dataset was \texttt{krum}/scaling, a \emph{different} cell, and that cell is explicitly not counted as a reversal. Cell~7 therefore holds the aggregator, the attack, the rung grid and the seed set fixed and changes only the dataset, to CIFAR-100 under the same architecture: a $100$-way task, which moves the backdoor's base rate and the clean-accuracy regime together. The outcome-gated ladder falls $-0.213$ $[-0.284,-0.141]$ where the instrument rises $+0.071$ $[+0.031,+0.110]$, opposite signs with both intervals excluding zero, so under the same frozen definition applied to every other row this is a \textbf{second sign reversal}. \textbf{It is a replication of the phenomenon, not an enlargement of the claim}: three datasets and two CNN configurations is the scope after it, the reversal is \emph{dataset-conditional} on exactly that evidence, and nothing here licenses a statement about a dataset or an architecture outside the three measured.

\textbf{Five disclosures about that cell, every one of them from the amendment rather than from hindsight.} (i) \textbf{The admissibility gate was two-sided and frozen before the run}: the identity rung had to reach mean clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$ \emph{and} mean ASR inside $[0.15,0.85]$, the upper bound because a saturated rung kills the \emph{rise} leg exactly as EMNIST's $0.027$ identity rung killed the \emph{fall} leg in the cell above. It passed on both sides ($0.393$ accuracy, $0.703$ ASR, means over seeds $42$--$46$), and the amendment named in advance the finding it would have reported instead: \emph{no third dataset within this compute budget admits this cell}. (ii) \textbf{No prediction was made, and the $\Delta\Lambda_a$ entry is absent rather than $0.0000$}. The rule was refuted by the EMNIST cell and withdrawn before this cell existed, so it generates nothing here, and \textbf{a dead mechanism is not revived by a cell that happens to agree with what it would have said}. (iii) \textbf{The cell is scored on the two endpoint rungs}, which is exactly what the frozen primary contrast reads, so the row is complete as printed. All four rungs of the frozen grid \emph{are} run, on both designs, at seeds $42$--$46$; \textbf{an earlier version of this disclosure said the interior rungs were not run and that the cell therefore carried no trend statistic, which was true when written and became false when the runner finished}, and we correct it here rather than silently. What the completed interior buys is a description and not a test: \textbf{the comparability pre-registration registers no trend secondary}, so the Jonckheere--Terpstra statistics that follow are \emph{post hoc}, they are emitted with \texttt{pre\_registered:~false}, and no verdict in Table~\ref{tab:sixcell} is a function of them. Both ladders start from the same identity rung and then move apart across every rung, not only at the top: the outcome-gated design falls monotonically ($0.7034$, $0.6804$, $0.6508$, $0.4905$; $J{=}32.0$, $z{=}{-}2.89$, $p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0019$) while the instrument rises monotonically ($0.7034$, $0.7035$, $0.7220$, $0.7740$; $J{=}96.0$, $z{=}{+}1.41$), so the reversal is a divergence of the whole ladder rather than an artifact of its endpoints. \textbf{The rising leg does not clear $0.05$ as a rank trend} ($p_{\uparrow}{=}0.079$, permutation $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.084$) even though its endpoint interval $[+0.031,+0.110]$ excludes zero, and \textbf{we report that weaker reading beside the stronger one} rather than the half that helps: at $n{=}5$ per rung a rise of this size is not separable from chance by ranks alone. Both $p_{\downarrow}$ values here are normal-approximation figures, following the supplement's §\ref{app:dose_detail}, because the permutation leg is computed for the increasing direction only. The CIFAR-10 cell of the same aggregator and attack has the same shape on both legs ($z{=}{-}2.96$, $p_{\downarrow}{=}1.5{\times}10^{-3}$ falling against $z{=}{+}1.48$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.069$ rising), under the same caveat on the rising one; its falling leg is the arm-4 trend already reported there, and it agrees because \texttt{analyze\_comparability.py} \emph{imports} that suite's trend test rather than reimplementing one. \textbf{Those four figures are at $n{=}5$, and stay there, even though that cell's endpoint row is now $n{=}20$}: the top-up ran the two \emph{endpoint} rungs only, so its four rungs hold $[20,5,5,20]$ seeds, and Jonckheere--Terpstra's null distribution is a function of those group sizes. A mixed-$n$ trend is therefore not the statistic this grid was described with. The scorer computes it anyway and prints it beside the quoted one so that its exclusion is visible rather than assumed ($z{=}{-}5.54$ falling, $z{=}{+}3.07$ rising); \textbf{we do not quote it}, and no verdict reads it. The quoted values are the trend over the five seeds all four rungs share, which is the pre-top-up block, so they are the same numbers the earlier draft printed and not a re-test on new data. (iv) \textbf{CIFAR-100 was chosen on wall time and we record that as the limitation it is}: at $660$~s per run it was the third dataset that fit an overnight budget, where EMNIST-byclass ($29.5$~h per cell, and an identity rung that fails the gate a priori) and ResNet18 ($65$~h) did not. \textbf{Those are \emph{comparability-cell} costs, two designs $\times$ four rungs $\times$ five seeds, and they do not price the single-design endpoint arm of §\ref{app:resnet18_dose}}, which is six runs and did fit one night; the figures are not in conflict because they cost different objects. This is the third dataset that fit, not the third that was most informative. (v) \textbf{The two designs coincide run for run at the identity rung of this cell}, bit-identically, as they also do for \texttt{coord\_median}/scaling and do \emph{not} for the EMNIST cell, so it is a property of the cell and not of the design. Both $\Delta$ estimates therefore share their $\kappa{=}0$ leg and the difference \emph{between} the two designs is carried entirely by the $\kappa{=}2$ rung, which is where the attenuation channel is open. We say so because two identical columns in an artifact look like duplicated data and are not.

\textbf{We report the refutation as a refutation.} $\Delta\Lambda_a$ does not explain when the two designs disagree. The four-cell separation that suggested it stands as a description of four cells and nothing more, and no threshold was fitted after the fact to rescue it. \textbf{The observation survives the mechanism's death}: across seven cells the two designs disagree on five, on two different attacks, three datasets and two CNN configurations, and two of those disagreements are sign reversals. What we do not have is an account of \emph{when} to expect it, which is the honest state of this result and a direct limit on how far the protocol of §\ref{sec:protocol} can be turned into a rule of thumb.

\textbf{The short form first}, because it is all the argument needs: we pre-registered a mechanism before the runs that tested it, one out-of-sample cell refuted it, and it is therefore withdrawn. \textbf{One process failure is disclosed below that, because it nearly reversed this section} and because a reader entitled to the first sentence is entitled to how nearly it went the other way. The EMNIST cell was first scored on non-identical seed sets ($n{=}5$ against a frozen $n{=}3$), the disagreement carried entirely by two seeds with no counterpart in the other design. We amended the pre-registration to complete the controlled ladder to the frozen seed set rather than resolve the cell on the three shared seeds, which give AGREE: choosing them would have chosen the answer. The completed comparison is the fully paired $n{=}5$ in the table and it still disagrees. That same amendment asserted that cross-design pairing was the convention the freeze had used. \textbf{It was not}, and what caught the error was not our judgement but the analyzer's own gate: under cross-design pairing the four \emph{published} cells stop reproducing (\texttt{krum}/scaling's controlled ladder moves $-0.010$ at $n{=}20$ to $-0.026$ at $n{=}5$), and the script refused to print. A third amendment withdraws the claim, and the full sequence is left in the pre-registration.


\section{The composition the screen cannot certify: FoolsGold$\to$RFA}
\label{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}

This section is the empirical witness for Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full}. FoolsGold$\to$RFA has the \emph{lowest observed committed ASR} of every composition evaluated here ($0.093$), and \textbf{our own criterion classifies it as a false negative}: neither constituent suppresses the attack alone (FoolsGold $0.732$, RFA $0.845$), so its suppression is \emph{emergent} in the sense of Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full} and C1 cannot hold. \textbf{We report it in detail because it is the sharpest illustration of the screen's scope, not because it is a flagship}: any criterion that certifies inherited suppression must miss compositions of this kind, which is what caps recall \emph{structurally}; with one emergent case among five low-ASR pairs that cap is $80\%$, and the $40\%$ we measure sits below it for reasons other than emergence. The characterization below is therefore descriptive (what this composition does, measured at $n{=}30$) and is not evidence for the criterion. \textbf{The $n{=}30$ measurement corrected our own earlier figure downward in reliability, and we report the correction rather than the settled number alone}: the per-seed distribution is right-skewed (median $0.060$, mean $0.093$, sample skew $+2.07$, $8/30$ seeds at or above $0.10$, maximum $0.380$), and seeds 42--46 fall in its left tail, so the $n{=}5$ value we first led with understated the pair by a factor of $1.8$. Any 5-seed figure for this composition should be read against that spread. We avoid calling it security-maximal: it also sustains the \emph{highest} ASR under our adaptive adversary ($0.167$, versus $0.045$ for the same pixel attack on the same five seeds and $0.064$ at $n{=}30$), and it carries a $50\%$ accuracy cost, so it is the right choice only where utility is nearly irrelevant. We validate at $n{=}30$ seeds with a full adaptive attack suite ($N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, 50 rounds), in Table~\ref{tab:fg_rfa_flagship}:

\textbf{Results.} Median ASR across all base attacks ($n{=}30$): $0.056$ [IQR $0.044$--$0.090$]; P95 worst-case: $0.253$ (committed\_scaling). \textbf{Three of the six adaptive strategies beat the base attacks and we lead with the strongest, not the median.} All six are measured at $n{=}5$ on seeds $42$--$46$, where the base composition reads $0.045$ (pixel), $0.051$ (scaling) and $0.050$ (oracle), so that is the seed-matched comparison. Against it, the criterion-aware joint-constraint adversary with decorrelation reaches mean $0.167$ at $\epsilon{=}1.0$ (median $0.135$, max $0.268$), which is $3.7\times$ the $0.045$ of the pixel arm it is built from and the ratio every other section of this paper quotes, and $0.130$ at $\epsilon{=}2.0$, and the projection adversary reaches $0.079$; the other three sit level with the base (the same criterion-aware attack without decorrelation $0.050$, Neurotoxin $0.048$, consensus-shift $0.045$). \textbf{So the composition does \emph{not} stay under $10\%$ against the strongest adversary we built}, and $0.167$ is the figure the opening paragraph reports. What survives is weaker and still worth stating: no adaptive \emph{mean} reaches the base composition's own P95 tail of $0.253$ (its worst single seed is $0.380$), though the $\epsilon{=}1.0$ maximum of $0.268$ does, and the $\epsilon{=}1.0$ gain costs the adversary nothing in clean accuracy ($0.562$ against $0.495$), so it is a real gain rather than a utility trade. The accuracy tradeoff ($0.50$ vs.\ $0.74$ for RFA alone) and Pareto alternatives are discussed below (Table~\ref{tab:pareto}).

\textbf{Composition-aware adversary (end-to-end).} We build an adversary that \emph{knows} the full FG$\to$RFA pipeline is deployed (Kerckhoffs) and optimizes against the complete composition, solving both constraints simultaneously: pairwise cosine similarity below FoolsGold's down-weighting threshold, \emph{and} $\ell_2$ distance to the geometric median below RFA's rejection radius. Any gradient-based optimizer faces the same tension (coordinating a backdoor raises FG-similarity, while staying near the median caps the perturbation and weakens the backdoor) and our analytical construction reaches the same boundary, the Weiszfeld iteration being contractive and the gradient at convergence determined by the final-iterate reweighting the joint constraint directly constrains.\footnote{A fully gradient-based optimizer differentiating through 50 Weiszfeld iterations would face identical binding constraints at convergence; the analytical formulation is equivalent without requiring a differentiable relaxation.} The ablation is informative: without decorrelation ASR stays at baseline ($0.050$), RFA-evasion alone failing because FoolsGold is the binding constraint; with it the adversary gains partial signal ($0.130$--$0.167$), which Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} explains as bounded, each adversary's block-restricted delta being too small to move the median outside the benign cluster.

\textbf{Contrast with rep$\to$tm.} FG$\to$RFA (median ASR $0.056$) provides near-absolute suppression; rep$\to$tm (oracle ASR $0.316$) provides strategic dominance against all tested adversary policies but does not suppress committed\_pixel ($0.679$). The two serve different roles.

\textbf{Accuracy--security tradeoff.} FG$\to$RFA achieves the lowest ASR of any tested composition but imposes a substantial clean-accuracy cost ($0.50$ vs.\ $0.74$ for RFA alone). This cost is intrinsic to FoolsGold's aggressive per-client downweighting, not to composition itself. Table~\ref{tab:pareto} places FG$\to$RFA on the accuracy--security Pareto frontier alongside alternative compositions:

The three composition rows are emitted by \texttt{experiments/analyze\_headline\_cis.py}, each at the seed count its own max-committed arm carries: FG$\to$RFA and Rep$\to$CM at $n{=}30$ (seeds $42$--$71$; Rep$\to$CM's is the pre-registered pixel top-up, $0.463$ $[0.412,0.514]$), FG$\to$CM at $n{=}5$ (seeds $42$--$46$). No \emph{composition} dominates another: FG$\to$CM buys $0.185$ accuracy for $+0.014$ ASR. The single-defense rows are listed for reference and are not all frontier points; \texttt{Rep alone} dominates \texttt{RFA alone} on both axes.}
