\caption{Per-seed NE, CIFAR-10 $N=100$ ($4{\times}5$ game). Seeds 43--46 identical.}
\label{tab:scale100}
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{c|c|l|l|c}
\toprule
Seed & Type & Adversary & Server & VoPD \\
\midrule
42      & Mixed & Pixel + Scaling & FedAvg + RFA & 0.012 \\
43--46  & Pure  & Model scaling   & RFA          & 0.000 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{ResNet18 architecture-replication mini-sweep: per-seed details}
\label{app:resnet18_replication_details}

Per-seed details for the ResNet18 architecture-replication mini-sweep behind the architecture-conditional collapse boundary in §\ref{sec:limitations}. \texttt{resnet18} with BatchNorm replaced by GroupNorm (8 groups), conv1 adapted to $32\times 32$ (kernel $3$, stride $1$, maxpool $\to$ Identity); 11.2M parameters. Identical FL parameters to cifar\_cnn: $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, $50$ rounds, $\tau=5$. Committed-scaling BR, 3 mix points $\times$ 5 seeds = 15 runs at $\sim 100$ min/run on MPS.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l|ccccc|c|c}
\toprule
mix (NC/rep) & s42 & s43 & s44 & s45 & s46 & ResNet18 mean & cifar\_cnn (R50) \\
\midrule
90/10 & 0.926 & 0.929 & 0.965 & 0.974 & 0.962 & $\mathbf{0.951 \pm 0.020}$ & $0.945 \pm 0.021$ \\
50/50 & 0.924 & 0.971 & 0.813 & 0.920 & 0.903 & $\mathbf{0.906 \pm 0.052}$ & $0.815 \pm 0.104$ \\
10/90 & 0.917 & 0.733 & 0.783 & 0.975 & 0.915 & $\mathbf{0.865 \pm 0.091}$ & $\mathbf{0.097 \pm 0.090}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

NC-dominant endpoint replicates within $0.01$; mid-mix is $\sim 9\%$ higher on ResNet18; the reputation-dominant endpoint shows an order-of-magnitude gap (no CI overlap). Seed 45's accuracy collapses to $0.153$ at NC10/rep90 (the persistent attack effectively breaks the model) while trigger ASR remains $0.975$. \textbf{Verdict: MEDIUM} (monotonic ordering preserved, endpoint criterion fails). The cifar\_cnn collapse boundary at $\sim 70$--$90\%$ reputation is therefore a property of the smaller-architecture regime. This sweep bounds the architecture-conditional claim of §\ref{sec:limitations} and establishes no boundary of its own; we draw no conclusion here about which mixture of the two defenses survives, since mixture policies are outside this paper's scope (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}).

\section{Is randomization itself costly? A compute-matched, pre-registered test}
\label{app:randomization}

\textbf{What this is, and what it is not.} This is a new experiment, pre-registered in
\texttt{experiments/}\allowbreak\texttt{pre\_registration\_randomized\_composition.md} and committed at
\texttt{e3fb038} before the first run. It is \emph{not} a reinstatement of the randomized-menu comparison
§\ref{sec:related} withdraws: that artifact stays withdrawn, because it is unpaired and
compute-confounded, and nothing below is scored against it. The question here is narrower and answerable:
holding defense computation fixed, does randomizing \emph{which} composition is applied each round cost
suppression relative to committing to the best member of the same set? The direction was fixed in advance
as $H_1: \bar{\Delta} > 0$, randomization \emph{hurts}, because that is the claim the withdrawn
comparison made and the claim we were asked to substantiate.

\textbf{Three defects in the predecessor, and what each fix costs.} (a) \emph{The arms were not
seed-matched.} The old runner tested \texttt{randomize\_order and rng.random() < 0.5}, which
short-circuits, so the fixed arm never drew that number and the randomized arm consumed one extra draw
per round from the same generator that selects participants. The arms therefore ran on divergent
participant streams and no paired test was licensed. Fixed with a separate \texttt{policy\_rng}, leaving
\texttt{rng} for participants only, which is why every arm here was re-run rather than partly reused.
(b) \emph{A defense-count confound.} Every \texttt{CM}$\to$\texttt{FG} round reduces to FoolsGold alone,
so the old gap conflated randomization with dropping a stage. Every member of every randomized set here
keeps FoolsGold as a genuine per-client upstream, so no round degenerates. (c) \emph{A mixed-$n$ trap.}
Every leg of every contrast below is recomputed per seed from
\texttt{results/}\allowbreak\texttt{randomized\_composition/}\allowbreak\texttt{summary.json} at seeds
42--46; the analyzer prints both legs' $n$ on every row and marks any mismatch.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
\toprule
arm & condition & def./rd & scaling ASR & pixel ASR & acc.\ (sc./px.) \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{fixed\_fg\_cm} & 2 & $0.1068 \pm 0.0306$ & $0.0988 \pm 0.0201$ & $0.682/0.664$ \\
1 & \texttt{fixed\_fg\_rfa} & 2 & $0.0510 \pm 0.0096$ & $0.0452 \pm 0.0118$ & $0.499/0.495$ \\
1 & \texttt{fixed\_fg\_tm} & 2 & $0.3843 \pm 0.2594$ & $0.2495 \pm 0.1026$ & $0.694/0.705$ \\
2 & \texttt{rand\_comp\_strong} & 2 & $\mathbf{0.1522 \pm 0.1091}$ & $\mathbf{0.1115 \pm 0.0440}$ & $0.607/0.598$ \\
2 & \texttt{rand\_comp\_all3} & 2 & $0.2317 \pm 0.2780$ & $0.0879 \pm 0.0301$ & $0.623/0.635$ \\
3$^{\dagger}$ & \texttt{temporal\_mix} & 1 & $0.5712 \pm 0.3737$ & $0.6600 \pm 0.1543$ & $\mathbf{0.307}/0.765$ \\
4$^{\dagger}$ & \texttt{pure\_coord\_median} & 1 & $0.5186 \pm 0.1413$ & $0.4425 \pm 0.1290$ & $0.764/0.767$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent All seven conditions, $n=5$ seeds 42--46, population sd. $^{\dagger}$\,\textbf{Compute matching
is claimed for arms 1--2 only}: arms 3 and 4 apply one defense per round and use roughly half the defense
computation, so they are reference points and not comparators. Arm 2's primary set is the \emph{smaller}
one, $\{$FG$\to$CM, FG$\to$RFA$\}$, whose members each suppress both committed attacks alone; FG$\to$TM
is a criterion-FAIL pair whose canonical scaling arm is $0.508 \pm 0.269$, above threshold, so a set
containing it could degrade merely because a non-suppressing member was drawn. That $0.508$ is the frozen
$n{=}3$ reading (seeds 42--44) and is \emph{not} the table's $0.3843$, which is the same arm at $n{=}5$;
the two agree exactly on the three shared seeds by control 3, and the difference is seeds 45--46 alone. Both sets were fixed by
rule in the pre-registration, not by any outcome measured here.

\textbf{Four positive controls, because this project has twice shipped a hook that silently never
fired.} (1) \emph{The hook fired}: every member of every randomized set appears in the stored per-round
policy log across all 15 streams. (2) \emph{The arms differ}: neither randomized arm is bit-identical to
any fixed arm at any seed, the closest approach being max per-seed $|d| = 0.2207$
(\texttt{rand\_comp\_strong}/scaling against \texttt{fixed\_fg\_cm}) and $0.0489$
(\texttt{rand\_comp\_all3}/pixel). (3) \emph{The harness did not change the run}: all eight per-seed
identity checks of the fixed arms against the canonical artifacts return $\max|d| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$
on their shared seeds, against \texttt{all\_compositions} (seeds 42--44) and \texttt{fg\_cm\_survivor}'s
\texttt{composed} (42--46). (4) \emph{The end-to-end path reproduces a published standalone}:
\texttt{pure\_coord\_median} returns $0.5186$ and $0.4425$ against
\texttt{wave2\_held\_out}'s \texttt{fedavg\_then\_coord\_median} at
$\max|d| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$, the same $n=5$ seeds. Controls 3 and 4 are what license comparing any
arm here to a number already in print.

\textbf{Control 1 fails as literally pre-registered, and we report it rather than restate it.} The
frozen assertion was a per-stream two-sided $99\%$ binomial interval with no family-wise correction. Three
of the 15 streams carry an excursion (four member-level flags):
\texttt{rand\_comp\_strong} seed 46 draws $36/14$ against $[16,34]$ (tail $P=0.00130$), and
\texttt{rand\_comp\_all3} draws $26$ at seed 45 and $27$ at seed 46 against $[8,25]$
($P=0.00492$, $P=0.00208$). Family-wise, $P(\text{at least one excursion}) = 1 - 0.99^{15} = 0.140$, so
excursions at this rate are expected. We do not treat this as invalidating, and say why: the failure mode
the control targets is a \emph{dead} hook, which yields a member with count $0$ or a $50/0$ log, and every
stream here draws every member. The pre-registered assertion is nonetheless the stricter one and it did
not hold on all streams; that is the honest statement. One further correction of the pre-registration's
own arithmetic: it wrote \texttt{rand\_comp\_all3}'s interval as approximately $8$--$26$, the exact
interval is $[8,25]$, and the exact one is used above.

\textbf{The primary contrast, paired per seed.} \texttt{rand\_comp\_strong} against the best member of
its own set, where ``best'' is fixed by rule as the lower full-artifact mean on that same attack, which
selects \texttt{fixed\_fg\_rfa} on both. On \emph{model scaling} the per-seed gaps are $+0.0352$,
$+0.2899$, $+0.1564$, $+0.0102$, $+0.0143$, giving $\bar{\Delta} = +0.1012$, $5/5$ concordant signs,
one-sided Wilcoxon $p=0.0312$ and paired $t=1.866$ ($p=0.0677$). On the \emph{pixel} backdoor they are
$+0.0816$, $+0.0428$, $+0.1581$, $+0.0133$, $+0.0356$, giving $\bar{\Delta} = +0.0663$, $5/5$ signs,
Wilcoxon $p=0.0312$ and $t=2.603$ ($p=0.0299$). At $n=5$ the minimum attainable Wilcoxon $p$ is
$1/32=0.031$, stated in advance, so only $5/5$ signs can reach $p<0.05$. Post hoc: the randomized arm
exceeds \emph{both} members, not just the better one, in $3$ of $5$ seeds on each attack, so the gap is
not interpolation between the two fixed arms. The secondary contrast, \texttt{rand\_comp\_all3} against
the best of all three arm-1 conditions, runs the same way: $\bar{\Delta} = +0.1806$ with $4/5$ signs on
scaling ($p=0.0625$) and $+0.0427$ with $4/5$ on pixel ($p=0.0625$).

\textbf{The pre-registered outcome: all three admissible outcomes obtained, on three different
objects.} Materiality required \emph{both} $\bar{\Delta} \geq 0.10$ and at least $4/5$ positive signs.
\emph{(i) Material, in the pre-registered direction, on model scaling}: $\bar{\Delta}=+0.1012$ with $5/5$
signs, so we state, scoped to this menu and this attack, that randomizing over non-degenerate
compositions costs suppression at matched defense computation. \emph{(ii) Not material on the pixel
backdoor}: $\bar{\Delta}=+0.0663$ clears the sign leg but not the magnitude leg, and the honest phrasing
at $n=5$ is that no material difference was detected and the smallest effect this design resolves is
$\approx 0.10$. \emph{(iii) The accuracy floor is breached by one arm}: \texttt{temporal\_mix} under model
scaling means $0.307$ clean accuracy against a frozen floor of $0.35$, so that cell is reported as
uninterpretable for ASR rather than compared, and every contrast involving it inherits the exclusion.
This is why arm 3 is a reference point in the table and carries no verdict.

\textbf{Three caveats, and what survives them.} First, $\bar{\Delta}$ clears the $0.10$ threshold by
$0.0012$ and is carried by two of five seeds ($+0.2899$ and $+0.1564$); on the three others the gap is
$\leq 0.036$. A threshold met by $1.2\%$ of itself is met, and it is also the weakest way a threshold can
be met, so we do not describe the scaling result as large. Second, the two tests disagree in opposite
directions across the two attacks: Wilcoxon reaches $p=0.0312$ on both while the $t$-test reaches
$p=0.0299$ on pixel, where materiality \emph{fails}, and only $p=0.0677$ on scaling, where it holds. The
stable part of the result is the sign structure, $10/10$ across both attacks, and not either magnitude.
Third, control 1 fails as literally pre-registered, as recorded above. What survives all three is the
weakest claim consistent with them and the one the design was built to license: at matched defense
computation and with the degeneracy confound removed, randomizing over a set of compositions did not
\emph{help} on either attack and was consistently worse on both, materially so on one. Nothing here
licenses a claim about randomized menus in general, about randomized \emph{internals} such as noise or
subsampling (L3), or about any menu but this one.

\end{document}
